  18: 
  19: ## What Is Citadel
  20: 
  21: An agent orchestration harness for Claude Code and OpenAI Codex. It coordinates multiple AI agents in parallel, persists memory across sessions, and routes your intent to the cheapest execution path automatically. Citadel adapts itself to each runtime: plugin-first for Claude Code, generated project artifacts plus Codex-native config for Codex.
  22: 
  23: ## Why Citadel Exists
  24: 
  25: **Without Citadel**, every agent session starts from zero. You re-explain architecture decisions. You re-discover that the auth module is fragile. You copy-paste the same review checklist. When a task is too big for one agent, you manually split it and lose context between the pieces. Your agents never get better at your codebase — you just get better at prompting them.
  26: 
  27: **With Citadel**, sessions resume where they left off. A `/do review` runs a structured 5-pass review that remembers what broke last time. A `/do overhaul the API layer` spawns parallel agents in isolated worktrees, shares discoveries between them, and merges the results. Skills you build once compound across every future session. The system learns from its own mistakes through campaign persistence and telemetry.
  28: 
  29: The difference: `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` tell the runtime about your project. Citadel gives the runtime the *infrastructure to work autonomously* — routing, memory, safety hooks, and coordination that a single guidance file can't provide.
  30: 
  31: ## Quickstart
  32: 
  33: **Prerequisites:** [Claude Code](https://docs.anthropic.com/en/docs/claude-code) or [Codex](https://github.com/openai/codex) + [Node.js 18+](https://nodejs.org/)
  34: 
  35: ```bash
  36: # 1. Clone Citadel
  37: git clone https://github.com/SethGammon/Citadel.git
  38: 
  39: # 2a. Claude Code runtime
  40: claude --plugin-dir /path/to/Citadel
  41: 
  42: # 2b. Codex runtime
  43: node /path/to/Citadel/scripts/codex-compat.js
  44: node /path/to/Citadel/scripts/install-hooks-codex.js
  45: codex
  46: 
  47: # 3. In either runtime, run setup
  48: /do setup
  49: 
  50: # 4. Try something
  51: /do review src/main.ts
  52: ```
  53: 
  54: [Quickstart for both runtimes](QUICKSTART.md) · [Claude Code installation guide](docs/CLAUDE_INSTALLATION_GUIDE.md) · [Codex installation guide](docs/CODEX_INSTALLATION_GUIDE.md)
  55: 
  56: ## How It Works
  57: 
  58: Say what you want. `/do` routes it to the cheapest tool that can handle it.
  59: 
  60: ```
  61: /do fix the typo on line 42        # Direct edit, no model call
  62: /do review the auth module         # 5-pass structured code review
  63: /do why is the API returning 500   # Root cause analysis
  64: /do build a caching layer          # Multi-step orchestrated build
  65: /do overhaul all three services    # Parallel fleet with isolated worktrees
  66: ```
  67: 
  68: Classification runs across four tiers, each cheaper than the last:
  69: 
  70: 1. **Pattern match** — catches trivial commands with regex. Zero tokens, zero model calls, instant.
  71: 2. **Session state** — checks if you're mid-campaign and resumes it. Still zero tokens.
  72: 3. **Keyword lookup** — scans your input against installed skill keywords ("review", "test", "refactor") and routes directly. Still zero tokens.
  73: 4. **LLM classification** — only when tiers 1-3 don't match, a structured complexity analysis (~500 tokens) determines whether you need a single-step Marshal, a multi-session Archon, or a parallel Fleet.
  74: 
  75: Most requests resolve at tiers 1-3 for free. Tier 4 is the exception, not the default. You never have to choose the tool.
  76: 
  77: **[See it route live](https://sethgammon.github.io/Citadel/)**
  78:

 101: 
 102: ## What You Get
 103: 
 104: **Cost transparency.** Citadel reads runtime-native session artifacts and computes real cost from API pricing. You see what every session, campaign, and agent actually costs. Use `/cost` for a full breakdown or `/dashboard` for the overview. A real-time tracker alerts you at configurable spend thresholds without interrupting your work.
 105: 
 106: **Safety hooks.** 32 hooks across 29 lifecycle events run automatically. A consent system gates external actions (pushes, PRs, comments) with first-encounter choice — always-ask, session-allow, or auto-allow. Protected branches can't be deleted. Path traversal and secrets exfiltration are blocked. A circuit breaker stops failure spirals before they burn tokens. All of this is configurable per-project in `harness.json`.
 107: 
 108: **Campaign persistence.** Multi-session work survives context compression and session boundaries. Start an architecture overhaul today, close your laptop, pick it up tomorrow — the campaign state, decisions, and progress are all preserved. `/do continue` resumes exactly where you left off.
 109: 
 110: **Parallel coordination.** Fleet mode spawns multiple agents in isolated git worktrees, shares discoveries between them in real time, and merges results. One command, multiple agents, no conflicts.
 111: 
 112: **Autonomous quality improvement.** `/evolve` is a research-driven improvement director that scores the harness against a rubric, forms causal hypotheses about why scores are low, validates them with scout agents before spending fleet budget, and runs improvement cycles until it hits a ceiling or budget. It accumulates a persistent belief model and transferable pattern library across sessions — so each run compounds on what prior runs learned.
 113: 
 114: ## FAQ
 115: 
 116: **Is this for me?** If you're running Claude Code or Codex on a real codebase and finding that agents lose context, repeat mistakes, or can't work in parallel, yes. If you're just starting out with either runtime, get a few sessions in first and come back when the friction shows up.
 117: 
 118: **How is this different from `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md`?** Those files tell the runtime about your project. Citadel tells the runtime *how to work*: durable state, intelligent routing, automated safety, and native parallelism — the infrastructure layer those files assume someone else built.
 119: 
 120: **Do I need to learn all 45 skills?** No. Just use `/do` and describe what you want in plain English. The router picks the right skill. You can go months without ever typing a skill name directly.
 121: 
 122: **What if `/do` routes to the wrong tool?** Tell it. "Wrong tool" or "just do it yourself" and it adjusts. You can also invoke any skill directly: `/review`, `/archon`, etc. The router is a convenience, not a gate.
 123: 
 124: **How much does it cost in tokens?** Citadel adds ~2.5% overhead to your session cost. Skills cost zero when not loaded. The `/do` router costs ~500 tokens only at Tier 4. Use `/cost` to see real token data and exact spend for any session or campaign.
 125: 
 126: **How is this different from CrewAI, LangChain, or Aider?** Those are agent frameworks: they give you primitives for building agents from scratch. Citadel is an *operating system for an existing agent* (Claude Code or Codex). You don't write agent code — you install a plugin and get routing, persistence, parallelism, and safety hooks on top of the agent you already use. If you're building a custom agent, use a framework. If you're using Claude Code or Codex and want it to work better, use Citadel.
 127: 
 128: **Does it work with OpenAI Codex?** Yes. The runtime layer (`packages/runtime-claude-code`, `packages/contracts`) abstracts over both runtimes. Skills, hooks, and campaigns are portable — the same `.planning/` state works whether you're running under Claude Code or Codex. Use `AGENTS.md` for Codex (parallel to `CLAUDE.md` for Claude Code).
 129: 
 130: **Does this work on Windows?** Yes. All hooks and scripts run on Node.js. The Claude plugin path and the Codex generated-artifact path both work cross-platform.

   1: ---
   2: name: evolve
   3: description: >-
   4:   Research-driven multi-cycle improvement director. Forms causal hypotheses
   5:   about why scores are low, validates them with scout agents before attacking,
   6:   dispatches axis-parallel fleet attacks, extracts transferable patterns, and
   7:   runs indefinitely within a budget envelope. Accumulates a persistent belief
   8:   model and pattern library across sessions.
   9: user-invocable: true
  10: auto-trigger: false
  11: last-updated: 2026-05-03
  12: ---
  13: 
  14: # /evolve — Improvement Director
  15: 
  16: ## Orientation
  17: 
  18: **Use when:** You want sustained autonomous quality advancement — the director
  19: forms hypotheses, scouts before attacking, and builds a belief model that
  20: compounds across cycles. Runs until a natural ceiling, budget exhaustion, or you
  21: say stop.
  22: 
  23: **Don't use when:** You want a single scored loop (`/improve`), a known axis
  24: attacked directly (`/improve --axis`), or a one-time audit (`/improve --score-only`).
  25: 
  26: **Key difference from `/improve`:** `/improve` follows the rubric mechanically.
  27: `/evolve` asks *why* scores are where they are, validates those theories before
  28: spending fleet budget, and extracts cross-skill patterns that propagate to skills
  29: never directly attacked.
  30: 
  31: ## Invocation
  32: 
  33: ```
  34: /evolve {target}                  # run until ceiling, velocity drop, or budget
  35: /evolve {target} --n={N}          # exactly N director cycles then stop
  36: /evolve {target} --budget=${X}    # run until cumulative spend reaches $X
  37: /evolve {target} --continue       # resume from saved director state
  38: /evolve {target} --status         # show belief model, velocity, spend — no attack
  39: /evolve {target} --axis={name}    # focus director on one axis (scout + attack only)
  40: ```
  41: 
  42: `target` maps to `.planning/rubrics/{target}.md`.
  43: If no rubric exists, run `/improve {target}` Phase 0 first — `/evolve` requires
  44: an approved rubric and will not auto-generate one.
  45: 
  46: ---
  47: 
  48: ## Campaign Artifacts
  49: 
  50: All findings are externalized incrementally — written after every phase, not
  51: only at cycle end. A crashed or compacted session resumes with full context.
  52: 
  53: | Artifact | Path | Contents |
  54: |---|---|---|
  55: | Director state | `.planning/evolve/{target}/director-state.json` | cycle count, spend, velocity history, current phase, halt status |
  56: | Belief model | `.planning/evolve/{target}/belief-model.jsonl` | one record per (axis, skill) per cycle: score, hypothesis, evidence, confidence |
  57: | Experiment log | `.planning/evolve/{target}/experiment-log.jsonl` | every experiment: hypothesis → prediction → actual delta → mechanism confirmed |
  58: | Pattern library | `.planning/evolve/{target}/pattern-library.md` | transferable patterns: what change to what axis class caused what delta in which skills |
  59: | Cycle digest | `.planning/evolve/{target}/cycle-{n}-digest.md` | human-readable per-cycle summary for review |
  60: | Global patterns | `.planning/research/patterns.md` | cross-target patterns written outside campaign scope; available to future sessions and other targets |
  61: | Knowledge wiki | `.planning/wiki/` | compiled wiki pages from `/learn`; integrates evolve discoveries across sessions |
  62: 
  63: Create `.planning/evolve/{target}/` on first invocation. Create `.planning/research/` if absent.
  64: 
  65: ---
  66: 
  67: ## Cycle Digest Format
  68: 
  69: ```markdown
  70: # Cycle {n} — {target} | {date}
  71: 
  72: ## Scores
  73: | Axis | Prior | This Cycle | Delta |
  74: 
  75: ## Hypotheses
  76: | ID | Axis | Hypothesis | Scout Result | Confidence |
  77: 
  78: ## What Was Attacked
  79: | Axis | Skill | Delta | Mechanism Confirmed |
  80: 
  81: ## Patterns Discovered This Cycle
  82: - {pattern}: {evidence}
  83: 
  84: ## Belief Model Updates
  85: - {hypothesis confirmed / rejected / revised}
  86: 
  87: ## Spend: ${cycle} this cycle | ${cumulative} cumulative | Velocity: {v}
  88: ```
  89: 
  90: ---
  91: 
  92: ## Director Cycle Protocol
  93: 
  94: ### Phase 1: Survey
  95: 
  96: Run `/improve {target} --score-only`. Record scores to belief model with delta
  97: from prior cycle (empty on cycle 1). Flag any axis that dropped since last cycle
  98: as `regression-watch` — these are checked first in Phase 2.
  99: 
 100: ### Phase 2: Hypothesize
 101: 
 102: For every axis below 8.0, generate one primary hypothesis in this form:
 103: 
 104: ```
 105: HYPOTHESIS: {axis} scores {n}/10 because {specific mechanism},
 106:             not because {common misread}.
 107: PREDICTION: Fixing {mechanism} will raise score ≥ {delta} across {N} skills.
 108: FALSIFICATION: If we apply {change} and score does not rise > 0.5, hypothesis rejected.
 109: ```
 110: 
 111: Draw hypotheses from: evaluator justifications in Phase 1, prior evidence in
 112: the belief model, and programmatic check failures. Do not hypothesize from score
 113: alone — the number is the symptom.
 114: 
 115: Write each hypothesis to the experiment log as `{ id, status: "pending", ... }`.
 116: 
 117: Skip hypothesis generation for an axis if the belief model already has a
 118: `confidence >= 0.8` confirmed hypothesis for it that has not yet been attacked.
 119: 
 120: ### Phase 3: Scout
 121: 
 122: For axes below 7.0, or axes with unconfirmed hypotheses: dispatch one scout
 123: agent per hypothesis. Scouts read — they do not modify files.
 124: 
 125: Each scout returns:
 126: ```json
 127: { "hypothesis_id": "...", "confirmed": true, "evidence": "...", "confidence": 0.85 }
 128: ```
 129: 
 130: **Scout confidence protocol**: Scouts read relevant files only — no edits, no test runs. Assign `confidence`:
 131: - **0.9+**: mechanism is directly observable (explicit absence, missing section, wrong value in file)
 132: - **0.7–0.89**: strong indirect evidence from 2+ corroborating observations
 133: - **0.4–0.69**: single observation that supports the hypothesis; alternative explanations plausible
 134: - **< 0.4**: no direct evidence found; hypothesis is speculative from this file set
 135: 
 136: Run scouts in parallel. Update experiment log:
 137: - `confidence >= 0.7` → `confirmed`
 138: - `confidence 0.4–0.69` → `needs-evidence` (do not attack; add to next cycle)
 139: - `confidence < 0.4` → `rejected`
 140: 
 141: Skip Phase 3 for any hypothesis already `confirmed` at `confidence >= 0.8` in
 142: the belief model from a prior cycle.
 143: 
 144: ### Phase 4: Prioritize
 145: 
 146: For each confirmed hypothesis compute:
 147: 
 148: ```
 149: EV = (delta_estimate × axis_weight × confidence) / (effort_tier × collision_multiplier)
 150: ```
 151: 
 152: - `effort_tier`: low=1.0, medium=1.5, high=2.5
 153: - `collision_multiplier`: 2.0 if axis shares primary files with another attack in this cycle
 154: 
 155: Select top K axes where K = min(confirmed count, 4). Document selection rationale
 156: in cycle digest. If `--axis` was set, skip ranking — attack only that axis.
 157: 
 158: ### Phase 5: Fleet Attack
 159: 
 160: Dispatch one agent per selected axis in an isolated worktree
 161: (Agent tool, `isolation: "worktree"`). Each agent receives:
 162: - The confirmed hypothesis and its falsification criterion
 163: - The specific files to modify
 164: - Verification oracle: `node scripts/run-with-timeout.js 300 node scripts/test-all.js`
 165: 
 166: Each agent returns a structured result:
 167: ```json
 168: {
 169:   "axis": "...", "skill": "...",
 170:   "delta": 1.2,
 171:   "mechanism_confirmed": true,
 172:   "files_changed": ["..."],
 173:   "approach": "..."
 174: }
 175: ```
 176: 
 177: **Merge rules:**
 178: - Non-conflicting worktrees: merge all
 179: - Conflicting worktrees (same file): keep higher delta, discard lower
 180: - Regression on any previously passing programmatic check: abort that worktree, do not merge
 181: - `mechanism_confirmed: false` (score improved but not via predicted mechanism): record as `incidental_improvement`, mark hypothesis as `needs-revision`
 182: 
 183: Commit each merged worktree with a message citing the hypothesis ID.
 184: 
 185: ### Phase 6: Synthesize
 186: 
 187: For each result:
 188: 1. Update belief model — append evidence record for (axis, skill)
 189: 2. Update experiment log — mark `verified` / `refuted` / `incidental`
 190: 3. Identify transferable patterns:
 191: 
 192: ```
 193: PATTERN: {axis_class} | Mechanism: {what caused improvement} | Delta: {avg} across {N} instances | Applies to: {skill list} | Confidence: high/medium/low
 194: ```
 195: 
 196: Write patterns to `.planning/evolve/{target}/pattern-library.md`.
 197: 
 198: **Compile into wiki:** After writing to the pattern library, call
 199: `/learn --from-evolve {target} --cycle {n}`. This compiles cycle discoveries
 200: into `.planning/wiki/` — integrating with findings from prior cycles and
 201: campaigns rather than siloing them in the evolve directory. Skip if `/learn`
 202: is not available in this session (log the skip, do not block the cycle).
 203: 
 204: ### Phase 7: Cross-Pollinate
 205: 
 206: For each `confidence: high` pattern, or any pattern confirmed in 2+ skills:
 207: apply to all other applicable skills as targeted single-file edits — without
 208: running a full attack cycle.
 209: 
 210: Run verification oracle per cross-pollinated skill. Commit only if all
 211: programmatic checks pass and no axis drops > 0.3. Revert on regression; mark
 212: pattern as `context-dependent`.
 213: 
 214: Write patterns that apply beyond this target to `.planning/research/patterns.md`.
 215: 
 216: ### Phase 8: Loop or Halt
 217: 
 218: Compute learning velocity:
 219: ```
 220: velocity = Σ(delta across all attacked axes this cycle) / axes_attacked
 221: ```
 222: Append to `director-state.json` velocity history.
 223: 
 224: **Halt conditions (check in order):**
 225: 1. `--n` cycles completed
 226: 2. `--budget` reached (cumulative cost ≥ limit)
 227: 3. All axes ≥ 9.0 across all scored skills
 228: 4. `velocity < 0.2` for 3 consecutive cycles AND no `needs-evidence` hypotheses remain
 229: 5. Level-up triggered (see below)
 230: 6. User says stop
 231: 
 232: **On velocity drop, before halting:** attempt one axis-class switch — attack the
 233: highest-EV axis from a category not touched in the last 2 cycles. If velocity
 234: is still < 0.2 after that cycle, halt.
 235: 
 236: **On level-up trigger** (no axis improved > 0.5 for 2 loops, ≥ 3 loops run,
 237: no programmatic failures): write level-up proposals to
 238: `.planning/rubrics/{target}-proposals.md`, set `status: level-up-pending` in
 239: director state, halt. The campaign resumes only after the human approves and
 240: edits the live rubric.
 241: 
 242: **On normal loop:** increment cycle, compress prior cycle findings to
 243: continuation context, return to Phase 1.
 244: 
 245: ---
 246: 
 247: ## Unlimited Mode
 248: 
 249: No `--n` and no `--budget` = unlimited. Declare before starting:
 250: 
 251: ```
 252: /evolve running in unlimited mode.
 253: Target: {target} | Exit: all axes ≥ 9.0 OR velocity < 0.2 for 3 cycles
 254: Estimated cost: $12–18/cycle | Spend so far: $0
 255: To halt after current cycle: type /stop or press Escape.
 256: ```
 257: 
 258: Every cycle, report:
 259: ```
 260: Cycle {n} complete. Spend: ${cycle} | Cumulative: ${total} | Velocity: {v}
 261: ```
 262: 
 263: When context approaches compression territory (session duration > 30 min or
 264: /compact recommended): write continuation checkpoint to director state,
 265: surface the `--continue` command. The next session picks up exactly where this
 266: one stopped.
 267: 
 268: For overnight / unattended runs: combine with `/daemon`. The director is
 269: daemon-compatible — daemon calls `/evolve {target} --continue` each session.
 270: Set `--budget` to cap total spend.
 271: 
 272: ---
 273: 
 274: ## Fringe Cases
 275: 
 276: - **`.planning/` does not exist**: error — run `/do setup` first to initialize the harness state directory, then retry.
 277: - **No rubric**: error — run `/improve {target}` Phase 0 first. List available targets in `.planning/rubrics/` as hint.
 278: - **No prior scores in belief model**: proceed from cycle 1; all deltas empty on first survey. Expected.
 279: - **All scouts return `needs-evidence`**: attack the top-EV axis anyway under `low-confidence` flag; record as exploratory. Mark result regardless.
 280: - **Scout agent hangs or times out** (dispatched scout never returns): After 10 minutes without a response, log the scout as `status: timed-out` in the experiment log with `confidence: 0`. Proceed with the remaining returned scouts. Never let a hung scout block the cycle — if all scouts time out, treat as "all scouts return needs-evidence" and attack the top-EV axis under `low-confidence` flag.
 281: - **All axes collide** (every axis shares files): serialize top 2 axes; parallelize remainder. Log collision.
 282: - **Cross-pollination causes regression**: revert that skill, mark pattern `context-dependent`, do not propagate further.
 283: - **Level-up mid-campaign**: pause, write proposals, set `level-up-pending`. `/evolve --continue` after human approval resumes cycle numbering from where it stopped.
 284: - **Budget overrun risk**: if projected spend for current cycle would exceed `--budget` by > 20%, warn and confirm before dispatching fleet.
 285: - **`--continue` with no director state**: error — no campaign to resume. Suggest `/evolve {target}` to start fresh.
 286: - **Pattern library > 50 entries**: consolidate — group by axis class, merge similar patterns, keep highest-confidence instance of each class. Log consolidation.
 287: - **Zero skills match target rubric**: error with message listing all `.planning/rubrics/*.md` targets.
 288: 
 289: ---
 290: 
 291: ## Quality Gates
 292: 
 293: - Every hypothesis must have an explicit falsification criterion before Phase 3
 294: - Scouts must run before fleet dispatch on any unconfirmed hypothesis
 295: - Belief model written after every phase, not only end of cycle
 296: - Cross-pollination requires passing verification oracle before commit
 297: - Regression on any previously-passing axis aborts that worktree commit
 298: - Pattern library and global patterns updated at every cycle end, even zero-improvement cycles
 299: - Cycle digest written even on abort or no-change cycles
 300: 
 301: ---
 302: 
 303: ## Contextual Gates
 304: 
 305: **Disclosure:**
 306: - State mode (unlimited / fixed / budget) and exit conditions before first cycle
 307: - Estimate $12–18 per full cycle before starting
 308: - Report spend and velocity at end of every cycle
 309: - Confirm before continuing if cumulative spend exceeds $50
 310: 
 311: **Reversibility:** Red. Cross-pollination modifies many files across the repo;
 312: level-up rewrites rubric anchors permanently. Each commit is individually
 313: revertable; high volume. Range: `git revert {first}^..{last}`.
 314: 
 315: **Trust gates:**
 316: - Novice (0-4 sessions): `--status` and `--n=1` only; unlimited blocked
 317: - Familiar (5-19): up to `--n=5`; unlimited requires explicit `--budget` cap
 318: - Trusted (20+): no cap; confirm if projected total > $100
 319: 
 320: ---
 321: 
 322: ## Exit Protocol
 323: 
 324: ```
 325: ---HANDOFF---
 326: - Target: {target} | Cycles: {n} | Spend: ${total} | Mode: {unlimited/n/budget}
 327: - Axes improved: {list with deltas}
 328: - Belief model: .planning/evolve/{target}/belief-model.jsonl ({N} confirmed, {M} rejected)
 329: - Pattern library: .planning/evolve/{target}/pattern-library.md ({N} patterns)
 330: - Global patterns: .planning/research/patterns.md
 331: - Knowledge wiki: .planning/wiki/index.md (compiled via /learn --from-evolve after each cycle)
 332: - Cycle digests: .planning/evolve/{target}/cycle-*-digest.md
 333: - Halt reason: {ceiling/velocity/budget/n-complete/user-stop/level-up-pending}
 334: - Level-up proposals: {path or N/A}
 335: - Reversibility: red — {N} commits across {M} files; revert range: git revert {range}
 336: - Recommended next: {level-up and re-run / new target / done}
 337: ---
 338: ```

   1: ---
   2: name: do
   3: description: >-
   4:   Unified router that auto-routes user intent to the right orchestrator or skill.
   5:   Classifies input by scope, complexity, persistence needs, and parallelism, then
   6:   dispatches to the cheapest path that can handle it: direct command, skill, marshal,
   7:   archon, or fleet. Single entry point for all work.
   8: user-invocable: true
   9: auto-trigger: false
  10: last-updated: 2026-03-20
  11: ---
  12: 
  13: # /do — Unified Intent Router
  14: 
  15: ## Orientation
  16: 
  17: Use `/do` when the user wants something done but doesn't know (or care) which tool handles it.
  18: **Don't use when:** you know the destination — invoke /marshal, /archon, /fleet, or any skill directly.
  19: 
  20: ## Commands
  21: 
  22: | Command | Behavior |
  23: |---|---|
  24: | `/do [anything]` | Classify intent, route to cheapest capable path |
  25: | `/do status` | Show full harness dashboard (/dashboard) |
  26: | `/do continue` | Resume most recent active campaign or fleet session |
  27: | `/do --list` | Show all skills grouped by category with trigger keywords |
  28: | `/do setup` | First-run experience — configure the harness for this project |
  29: 
  30: ## Protocol
  31: 
  32: Classification runs top-to-bottom. First match wins. Each tier is cheaper than the next.
  33: 
  34: ### Step 0: Skill Registry Check (Cost: ~0 on hit | ~50 tokens on miss)
  35: 
  36: Before routing, check if new skills have been added since last registration.
  37: 
  38: 1. Count installed Citadel skills (built-in from plugin + custom in project's `.claude/skills/`)
  39: 2. Read `registeredSkillCount` from `.claude/harness.json`
  40: 3. **If counts match**: continue to Tier 0. Zero cost.
  41: 4. **If counts differ** (or harness.json doesn't exist yet):
  42:    a. Read the `registeredSkills` array from harness.json (default: `[]`)
  43:    b. Diff skill names against the registered list
  44:    c. For each unknown skill: read ONLY lines 1-10 of its `SKILL.md` (frontmatter)
  45:    d. Extract `name` and `description` from frontmatter
  46:    e. Add the skill to the Tier 2 keyword table for this session using its
  47:       `name` and `description` words as match targets
  48:    f. Log to the user: `"Discovered {N} new skill(s): {names}. Run /do setup to permanently register routing keywords."`
  49:    g. Update `registeredSkillCount` and `registeredSkills` array in harness.json
  50: 
  51: **This means:**
  52: - 99% of invocations: one number comparison, zero file reads
  53: - New skill dropped in: reads only the new frontmatter, routes immediately
  54: - `/do setup` does a full registry rebuild with permanent keyword assignment
  55: 
  56: ### Tier 0: Pattern Match (Cost: ~0 tokens | Latency: <1ms)
  57: 
  58: Regex/keyword on raw input. Catches trivial commands:
  59: 
  60: | Pattern | Action |
  61: |---|---|
  62: | "typecheck" or "type check" | Run the project's typecheck command |
  63: | "build" | Run the project's build command |
  64: | "test" or "tests" | Run the project's test command |
  65: | "status", "dashboard", "what's happening", "what's going on", "show activity" | Show full harness dashboard (/dashboard) |
  66: | "continue" or "keep going" | Resume active campaign or fleet session |
  67: | "setup" | Run `/do setup` first-run experience |
  68: | "--list" or "list" | Show all available skills |
  69: | "fix typo in X" or "rename X to Y" | Direct edit (no orchestrator needed) |
  70: | "commit" | Stage and commit changes |
  71: | "rollback", "undo phase", "restore checkpoint" | Find active campaign, read latest checkpoint ref, run git stash pop |
  72: 
  73: If matched → execute directly. Done.
  74: 
  75: ### Tier 1: Active State Short-Circuit (Cost: ~0 tokens | Latency: <100ms)
  76: 
  77: Check for active campaigns or fleet sessions that match the input scope:
  78: 
  79: 1. Read `.planning/campaigns/` for files with `Status: active` or `status: active` in frontmatter
  80: 2. Read `.planning/fleet/` for session files with `status: active` or `needs-continue`
  81: 3. **Improve campaigns (type: improve):** if the active campaign has `type: improve` in
  82:    frontmatter, route to `/improve {target} --continue` where `{target}` is the campaign's
  83:    `target` field. Do NOT route improve campaigns to archon -- improve is its own orchestrator.
  84: 4. If input scope matches a non-improve active campaign → `/archon continue`
  85: 5. If fleet session needs continuation → `/fleet continue`
  86: 6. If input mentions a campaign by name → resume it (check type field for routing)
  87: 7. **If input is "continue" but NO active campaign or fleet session found:**
  88:    - Output: "No active campaign or fleet session found. Nothing to continue."
  89:    - **If `.planning/daemon.json` exists with `status: "running"`:** the daemon spawned
  90:      this session but there's no work to do. Update daemon.json:
  91:      `status: "stopped"`, `stopReason: "no-active-work"`,
  92:      `stoppedAt: "{ISO timestamp}"`. Delete both triggers if IDs are present.
  93:      Output: "[daemon] Stopped -- no active campaign found. The work is done."
  94:    - Exit. Do NOT fall through to Tier 2 or 3.
  95: 
  96: If matched → resume the active work. Done.
  97: 
  98: ### Tier 2: Skill Keyword Match (Cost: ~0 tokens | Latency: <10ms)
  99: 
 100: Match input against installed skill keywords from Citadel's built-in skills
 101: and any project-level custom skills in `.claude/skills/`.
 102:

 141: | "organize", "directory structure", "folder structure", "project structure", "file organization", "where should this go", "cleanup directories" | `/organize` |

 148: 
 149: If ONE skill matches with high confidence → invoke it directly. Done.
 150: High confidence = evaluator assigns ≥ 0.85 probability to exactly one skill. Below 0.85, or multiple skills above 0.70, fall through to Tier 3.
 151: If MULTIPLE skills match → carry the candidate set to Tier 3. Tier 3 disambiguates between candidates only, not from scratch. Tie-break: prefer the candidate with fewer trigger keywords.
 152: 
 153: ### Tier 3: LLM Complexity Classifier (Cost: ~500 tokens | Latency: ~1-2s)
 154: 
 155: When Tiers 0-2 don't resolve, classify across 6 dimensions:
 156: 
 157: ```
 158: SCOPE: single-file | single-domain | cross-domain | platform-wide
 159: COMPLEXITY: 1 (trivial) | 2 (simple) | 3 (moderate) | 4 (complex) | 5 (campaign)
 160: INTENT: fix | build | create | add | audit | redesign | research | improve | wire | prune
 161: REQUIRES_PERSISTENCE: true | false (multi-session?)
 162: REQUIRES_PARALLEL: true | false (independent sub-tasks?)
 163: REQUIRES_TASTE: true | false (quality judgment beyond tests?)
 164: ```
 165: 
 166: **Routing rules (first match wins):**
 167: 
 168: | Condition | Route |
 169: |---|---|
 170: | INTENT is "create", Complexity >= 3 | `/create-app` |
 171: | INTENT is "create", Complexity <= 2 | `/scaffold` |
 172: | INTENT is "add", existing source files present | `/create-app` (Tier 5 — feature mode) |
 173: | INTENT is "add", no existing source files | `/scaffold` |
 174: | Complexity 1, single skill match | Skill directly |
 175: | Complexity 1, no skill match | Do it yourself (direct edit) |
 176: | Complexity 2, single domain | `/marshal` |
 177: | Complexity 2-3, known skill domain | Skill, with marshal fallback |
 178: | Complexity 3, cross-domain | `/marshal` |
 179: | Complexity 3-4, requires persistence | `/archon` |
 180: | Complexity 4, requires taste/judgment | `/archon` |
 181: | Complexity 4-5, requires parallel | `/fleet` |
 182: | Complexity 5, platform-wide | `/fleet` |
 183: | Confidence < 0.7 | `/marshal` (safe default) |
 184: 
 185: **Important:** A repeated pattern complaint ("I keep doing X manually", "the agent
 186: always makes this mistake") should route to `/create-skill`. A repeated pattern
 187: is a skill waiting to be extracted.
 188: 
 189: ### Step 3.5: Proportionality Check
 190: 
 191: After classification and before execution, verify the response is proportional to the input:
 192: 
 193: **Downgrade triggers (apply in order):**
 194: 
 195: | Condition | Action |
 196: |---|---|
 197: | Input < 20 words AND routed to Archon or Fleet | Downgrade to Marshal. Log: "Input too brief for campaign-level orchestration." |
 198: | Input mentions a single file AND routed to Fleet | Downgrade to Marshal or skill. Log: "Single-file scope doesn't warrant parallel agents." |
 199: | Estimated sessions > 5 AND user is Novice trust level | Cap at 3 sessions. Log: "Capping sessions for novice user. Run more to unlock higher budgets." |
 200: | Routed to Daemon AND user is Novice trust level | Block. Output: "Daemon mode requires familiarity with the harness. Complete a few sessions first." |
 201: | Estimated cost > $50 AND no explicit budget flag | Confirm with user regardless of trust level. |
 202: 
 203: **Upgrade triggers:**
 204: 
 205: | Condition | Action |
 206: |---|---|
 207: | Input complexity >= 4 AND routed to a bare skill | Suggest Marshal. "This looks complex enough for orchestration. Route to /marshal instead?" |
 208: | Input mentions "overnight" or "continuous" AND routed to Archon | Suggest daemon. "This sounds like continuous work. Want to run it as a daemon?" (skip if Novice) |
 209: | Input contains 2+ clearly independent tasks AND complexity >= 3 | Run Fleet auto-decomposition (see below). |
 210: 
 211: **Fleet auto-decomposition — 1/2/3 confirmation prompt:**
 212: 
 213: When 2+ independent tasks detected (non-overlapping scopes, complexity >= 3, not already routed to full Fleet), read `consent.fleetSpawn` from harness.json:
 214: - `auto-allow` → route directly to `/fleet --quick`
 215: - `always-ask` or `null` → show prompt: "These look independent — run in parallel? [1=yes  2=always  3=no]"
 216:   - 1: route to `--quick`, preference unchanged
 217:   - 2: route to `--quick`, write `writeConsent('fleetSpawn', 'auto-allow')`
 218:   - 3: run sequentially; if "don't ask again", write `always-ask`
 219: 
 220: `readConsent`/`writeConsent` are in `hooks_src/harness-health-util.js`.
 221: 
 222: **Trust level:** Read from `harness.json` `trust` object. Levels: novice (0-4 sessions), familiar (5-19), trusted (20+ with 2+ campaigns). `trust.override` takes precedence.
 223: 
 224: ### Step 4: After Classification
 225: 
 226: 1. **Log routing decision** (fire-and-forget):
 227:    `node .citadel/scripts/telemetry-log.cjs --event agent-complete --agent do-router --session routing --status success --meta '{"tier":N,"target":"[skill]","input_chars":M}'`
 228: 
 229:    Use `.citadel/scripts/telemetry-log.cjs` (the project-local copy). If it doesn't exist, skip logging silently — never block routing on telemetry failure.
 230: 
 231: 2. **Announce the routing decision**: "Routing to [target] because [one-sentence reason]"
 232: 3. **Invoke the target** skill or orchestrator
 233: 4. If the target fails or the user says "wrong tool", try the next tier up. If the target is already Tier 3 (marshal fails or user explicitly escalates from a failed marshal attempt): re-route to `/archon` with the original input as context.
 234: 
 235: ## /do --list
 236: 
 237: Output a grouped skill list drawn from the system reminder's available skills. Group by category (Orchestration, App Creation, Code Quality, Research & Debugging, GitHub & CI, Infrastructure, Monitoring, Utilities, Observability). For each skill, show `/name  — one-line description`. Include a footer: "Direct invocation (/skill-name) always bypasses the router."
 238: 
 239: ## Fringe Cases
 240: 
 241: - **`.planning/` does not exist**: The router works without `.planning/`. Tiers 0, 2, and 3 are fully independent of it. Tier 1 (active-state short-circuit) reads `.planning/campaigns/` and `.planning/fleet/` — if those directories are absent, skip Tier 1 gracefully and fall through to Tier 2. Never crash on a missing `.planning/` directory.
 242: - **`harness.json` missing**: Skip the Skill Registry Check and proceed directly to Tier 0. Announce discovered skills from the filesystem if counts can be read, otherwise route from built-in keywords.
 243: - **Multiple skills match at Tier 2**: Carry candidates to Tier 3 per Tier 2 disambiguation rule above.
 244: - **User input is empty or whitespace**: Respond with the `--list` output and a prompt to provide a direction.
 245: - **Routed skill not found**: Report "Skill not found" and fall back to Marshal as the safe default.
 246: 
 247: ## Contextual Gates
 248: 
 249: **Disclosure:** "Routing to [skill]. See that skill's contextual gates for reversibility."
 250: **Reversibility:** depends on routed skill — check the routed skill's reversibility
 251: **Trust gates:**
 252: - Any: routing and dispatch; inherits trust gates from the routed skill.
 253: 
 254: ## Quality Gates
 255: 
 256: - Tier 0-2 must resolve in under 1 second
 257: - Tier 3 classification must be transparent (announce reasoning)
 258: - Never route a trivial task (complexity 1) to Archon or Fleet
 259: - Never route a multi-session task to a bare skill
 260: - If routing fails, default to Marshal (safe middle ground)
 261: 
 262: ## Exit Protocol
 263: 
 264: After routing and execution complete:
 265: - If the routed skill/orchestrator produces a HANDOFF, relay it to the user
 266: - If the task was trivial (Tier 0), just show the result
 267: - Do not add overhead to simple tasks

   1: ---
   2: name: fleet
   3: description: >-
   4:   Parallel campaign orchestrator. Runs multiple campaigns in coordinated waves
   5:   within a single session. Spawns 2-3 agents per wave in isolated worktrees,
   6:   collects discoveries, shares context between waves. Use when work decomposes
   7:   into 3+ independent streams that can run simultaneously.
   8: user-invocable: true
   9: auto-trigger: false
  10: last-updated: 2026-03-21
  11: ---
  12: 
  13: # /fleet — Parallel Coordinator
  14: 
  15: Use for 3+ independent work streams that can run simultaneously in isolated worktrees. Do NOT use for single-file scope, linear work, or when a marshal or skill suffices.
  16: 
  17: ## Orientation
  18: 
  19: **Use when:** Running 2+ independent work streams in parallel — tasks with non-overlapping file scopes that can execute simultaneously.
  20: 
  21: **Don't use when:** Work must execute sequentially or accumulate findings across phases (use `/archon`), a single orchestrated session is enough (use `/marshal`), or the task is simple enough for a bare skill.
  22: 
  23: ## Commands
  24: 
  25: | Command | Behavior |
  26: |---|---|
  27: | `/fleet [direction]` | Decompose direction into parallel streams, execute in waves |
  28: | `/fleet [path-to-spec]` | Read a spec file, decompose into streams |
  29: | `/fleet continue` | Resume from the last fleet session file |
  30: | `/fleet` (no args) | Health diagnostic → work queue → execute |
  31: | `/fleet --quick [task1]; [task2]` | Lightweight parallel mode for solo devs — 2+ tasks, single wave, auto-merge, no session file |
  32: | `/fleet --speculative N [direction]` | Try N different approaches to the same task in parallel — see Speculative Mode below |
  33: 
  34: ## Protocol
  35: 
  36: ### Step 1: WAKE UP
  37: 
  38: 1. Read CLAUDE.md (project conventions)
  39: 2. Check `.planning/campaigns/` for active campaigns
  40: 3. Check `.planning/coordination/claims/` for external claims
  41: 4. Determine input mode: directed, spec-driven, continuing, or undirected
  42: 5. **Load prior session context**: If `.planning/momentum.json` exists, run
  43:    ```bash
  44:    node .citadel/scripts/momentum-read.cjs
  45:    ```
  46:    and read the output. Use the active scopes and recurring decisions to inform
  47:    work queue prioritization. Skip silently if the file is absent or output is empty.
  48: 
  49: > **Wave context restoration:** Use the Claude Code Compaction API to restore fleet
  50: > session context at the start of each session. Do NOT read `.claude/compact-state.json`
  51: > — that pattern is deprecated in favour of server-side compaction (available on Opus 4.6+).
  52: > Fleet session files (`.planning/fleet/session-{slug}.md`) remain the source of truth
  53: > for inter-wave discovery relay; compaction handles agent memory, not campaign state.
  54: > If the Compaction API is unavailable, fall back to reading the fleet session file's
  55: > Continuation State directly.
  56: 
  57: ### Step 1b: LOG SESSION START + START WATCHER
  58: 
  59: ```bash
  60: node .citadel/scripts/telemetry-log.cjs --event campaign-start --agent fleet --session {session-slug}
  61: node .citadel/scripts/momentum-watch-start.cjs
  62: ```
  63: 
  64: The watcher runs in the background and re-synthesizes `momentum.json` within 500ms of any new discovery write. Safe to call if already running — only one watcher runs per project.
  65: 
  66: ### Step 2: WORK QUEUE
  67: 
  68: Produce a ranked list of campaigns with:
  69: 
  70: | Column | Purpose |
  71: |---|---|
  72: | Campaign name | What this stream does |
  73: | Scope | Which directories it touches |
  74: | Dependencies | What must complete before this can start |
  75: | Wave | Which wave to assign it to |
  76: | Agent type | What kind of agent to spawn |
  77: 
  78: **Rules for work queue:**
  79: - Independent items go in Wave 1
  80: - Items that depend on Wave 1 results go in Wave 2
  81: - Maximum 3 agents per wave (conservative default)
  82: - Scope must NOT overlap between agents in the same wave
  83: 
  84: ### Step 3: WAVE EXECUTION
  85: 
  86: For each wave:
  87: 
  88: 1. **Prepare context** for each agent:
  89:    - CLAUDE.md content
  90:    - `.claude/agent-context/rules-summary.md`
  91:    - **Map slice** (if `.planning/map/index.json` exists): run
  92:      `node scripts/map-index.js --query "<agent's scope keywords>" --max-files 15`
  93:      and inject the results as a `=== MAP SLICE ===` block. If the index does
  94:      not exist, skip silently.
  95:    - **Prior session context** (all waves): re-read `momentum.json` fresh at each
  96:      wave boundary via `node .citadel/scripts/momentum-read.cjs` and inject as a
  97:      `=== PRIOR SESSION CONTEXT ===` block. Re-reading (rather than reusing the
  98:      Step 1 snapshot) picks up discoveries written by parallel Fleet sessions in
  99:      other terminals. If the output is empty, skip silently.
 100:    - Campaign-specific direction and scope
 101:    - Discovery briefs from previous waves (if any)
 102: 
 103: 2. **Log wave start**:
 104:    ```bash
 105:    node .citadel/scripts/telemetry-log.cjs --event wave-start --agent fleet --session {session-slug} --meta '{"wave":N,"agents":["name1","name2"]}'
 106:    ```
 107: 
 108: 3. **Spawn agents** with `isolation: "worktree"`:
 109:    ```
 110:    Agent(
 111:      prompt: "{full context + direction}",
 112:      isolation: "worktree",
 113:      mode: "bypassPermissions"
 114:    )
 115:    ```
 116: 
 117: 4. **Collect results** from all agents in the wave
 118: 
 119: 4.5. **Validate wave results** — spawn one Phase Validator per agent in parallel
 120:    (validators are Haiku, read-only, effort: low):
 121:    ```
 122:    Agent(
 123:      subagent_type: "citadel:phase-validator",
 124:      prompt: "Campaign: {session-slug}. Wave {N} agent: {agent-name}.
 125:               Exit conditions: {agent's scope goal and any stated conditions}.
 126:               HANDOFF: {agent's full handoff text}",
 127:      effort: "low"
 128:    )
 129:    ```
 130:    Collect all validator verdicts. For each agent:
 131:    - **`verdict: "pass"`**: mark agent `validated` in session file. Proceed.
 132:    - **`verdict: "fail"`**: check retry counter for this agent (max 2 retries in fleet;
 133:      single-session so lower budget than Archon's 3):
 134:      - **Retries remain**: re-spawn the failed agent in a new worktree with the
 135:        validator's `conditions_failed` and `suggestions` appended to its prompt.
 136:        Collect its result and re-validate. Decrement counter.
 137:      - **Retries exhausted**: mark agent `partial` in session file. Log
 138:        `validator_halt: {agent-name} wave {N} — {conditions_failed}`. Continue.
 139:    - **Validator timeout or unparseable output**: treat as pass with warning. Log. Advance.
 140: 
 141:    Run all validators for the wave in a single parallel batch — do not validate
 142:    sequentially. The cost is proportional to the wave size, not multiplicative.
 143: 
 144: 5. **Log per-agent results**:
 145:    ```bash
 146:    node .citadel/scripts/telemetry-log.cjs --event agent-complete --agent {agent-name} --session {session-slug} --status {success|partial|failed}
 147:    ```
 148: 
 149: 6. **Compress discoveries** for each agent:
 150:    - Extract HANDOFF blocks
 151:    - Run `node .citadel/scripts/compress-discovery.cjs` on each output
 152:    - Write compressed briefs to `.planning/fleet/briefs/`
 153: 
 154: 6b. **Write persistent discovery records** for each agent (cross-session memory):
 155:    ```bash
 156:    node .citadel/scripts/discovery-write.cjs \
 157:      --session {session-slug} \
 158:      --agent {agent-name} \
 159:      --wave {wave-number} \
 160:      --status {success|partial|failed} \
 161:      --scope "{comma-separated-scope-dirs}" \
 162:      --handoff "{json-array-of-handoff-items}" \
 163:      --decisions "{json-array-of-decisions}" \
 164:      --files "{json-array-of-files-touched}" \
 165:      --failures "{json-array-of-failures}"
 166:    ```
 167: 
 168: 7. **Log wave complete**:
 169:    ```bash
 170:    node .citadel/scripts/telemetry-log.cjs --event wave-complete --agent fleet --session {session-slug} --meta '{"wave":N,"status":"complete"}'
 171:    ```
 172: 
 173: 8. **Merge branches** from worktrees:
 174:    - Review changes from each agent
 175:    - If clean merge: merge the branch
 176:    - If conflicts: record in session file, then decide:
 177:      - **Resolve if:** the conflict is < 20 lines and affects only formatting or naming
 178:      - **Skip if:** the conflict involves competing logic changes; keep the higher-delta worktree result and log the discarded changes in session file
 179: 
 180: 9. **Update session file** with wave results and accumulated discoveries
 181: 
 182: ### Step 5: COMPLETION
 183: 
 184: After all waves:
 185: 
 186: 1. Run typecheck on the full project via `node scripts/run-with-timeout.js 300 <typecheck-cmd>`
 187: 2. Run tests if configured (also use the timeout wrapper). If tests fail after wave completion: apply the same error ladder as the main protocol — 1-2 failures: fix before merging; 3-4 failures: attempt fixes, continue if resolved; 5+ failures: halt the wave merge for that worktree and log `wave_test_fail: true` in the session file.
 188: 3. Update session file status to `completed`
 189: 4. Log session completion:
 190:    ```bash
 191:    node .citadel/scripts/telemetry-log.cjs --event campaign-complete --agent fleet --session {session-slug}
 192:    ```
 193: 5. **Update momentum** (cross-session synthesis):
 194:    ```bash
 195:    node .citadel/scripts/momentum-synthesize.cjs
 196:    ```
 197: 5.5. **Propagate knowledge** — for each campaign that completed this session, run:
 198:    ```bash
 199:    npm run propagate -- --campaign {slug}
 200:    ```
 201:    Run once per completed campaign slug (not per wave). If multiple campaigns
 202:    completed, run for each slug. If `npm run propagate` is unavailable, note each
 203:    slug in the fleet session file under `## Pending Propagation`.
 204: 6. Output final HANDOFF
 205: 
 206: ## Fleet Session File Format
 207: 
 208: Create at `.planning/fleet/session-{slug}.md`:
 209: 
 210: ```markdown
 211: # Fleet Session: {name}
 212: 
 213: Status: active | needs-continue | completed
 214: Started: {ISO timestamp}
 215: Direction: {original direction}
 216: 
 217: ## Work Queue
 218: | # | Campaign | Scope | Deps | Status | Wave | Agent |
 219: 
 220: ## Wave N Results
 221: ### Agent: {name}
 222: **Status:** complete | partial | failed
 223: **Built:** ...  **Decisions:** ...  **Files:** ...
 224: 
 225: ## Shared Context (Discovery Relay)
 226: - {cross-agent finding → what Wave N+1 should know}
 227: 
 228: ## Continuation State
 229: Next wave: N  Blocked items: ...  Auto-continue: true
 230: ```
 231: 
 232: ## Scope Overlap Prevention
 233: 
 234: Before assigning agents to a wave:
 235: 
 236: 1. List all scope directories for each agent
 237: 2. Check for parent/child overlaps:
 238:    - `src/api/` and `src/api/auth/` OVERLAP (parent/child)
 239:    - `src/api/` and `src/ui/` do NOT overlap (siblings)
 240: 3. `(read-only)` scopes never conflict
 241: 4. If overlap: move one agent to a later wave
 242: 
 243: Also check `.planning/coordination/claims/` for external claims.
 244: 
 245: ## Budget Management
 246: 
 247: **Effort hints for wave agents** (use the `effort` parameter, not `budget_tokens`):
 248: 
 249: | Agent Type | Effort | ~Tokens |
 250: |---|---|---|
 251: | Fleet scouts (research, mapping, audit) | `medium` | ~100K each |
 252: | Execution agents (build, refactor, implement) | `high` | ~250K each |
 253: | Verify agents (typecheck, visual-verify, QA) | `low` | ~60K each |
 254: 
 255: The `effort` parameter is GA as of April 2026 and produces ~20–40% token reduction
 256: compared to manually tuned `budget_tokens` values. Always prefer `effort` for new wave definitions.
 257: 
 258: ## Quality Gates
 259: 
 260: - All agents must receive full context injection
 261: - Scope must not overlap between same-wave agents
 262: - Every wave must produce compressed discovery briefs
 263: - Discovery relay must be injected into subsequent waves
 264: - Merge conflicts must be resolved or explicitly recorded
 265: - Final typecheck must pass after all waves
 266: 
 267: ## Agent Timeouts
 268: 
 269: Sub-agents can hang indefinitely on tool calls. Fleet must enforce execution time limits
 270: at the orchestrator level.
 271: 
 272: ### Default Timeouts
 273: 
 274: | Agent Type | Default Timeout | Override Key |
 275: |---|---|---|
 276: | Skill-level agents | 10 minutes | `agentTimeouts.skill` |
 277: | Research scouts | 15 minutes | `agentTimeouts.research` |
 278: | Build agents | 30 minutes | `agentTimeouts.build` |
 279: 
 280: Timeouts are configurable in `harness.json`:
 281: ```json
 282: {
 283:   "agentTimeouts": {
 284:     "skill": 600000,
 285:     "research": 900000,
 286:     "build": 1800000
 287:   }
 288: }
 289: ```
 290: 
 291: ### Timeout Protocol
 292: 
 293: On timeout: log `agent-timeout` event, extract partial HANDOFF if present, retry once with simplified prompt (Wave 1 critical scope only), skip otherwise. Never block the wave. Record `Status: timed out` in session file.
 294: 
 295: Read timeout values from `harness.json` → `agentTimeouts.{skill|research|build}` (defaults: 600000/900000/1800000ms).
 296: 
 297: ## Shared State Merge Strategies
 298: 
 299: Parallel agents writing to the same `.planning/` directory can silently overwrite
 300: each other. Each shared resource has a declared merge strategy:
 301: 
 302: | Resource | Strategy | Rule |
 303: |---|---|---|
 304: | `.planning/discoveries/*.md` | append-only | Never overwrite an existing discovery file. Each agent writes its own uniquely-named file: `{agent-id}-{timestamp}.md`. |
 305: | `.planning/fleet/briefs/*.md` | append-only | Each agent writes its own brief. Fleet coordinator reads all of them. |
 306: | `.planning/fleet/session-{slug}.md` | lock-on-write | Only the Fleet coordinator updates the session file. Agents never write to it directly — they emit HANDOFFs that the coordinator reads and transcribes. |
 307: | `.planning/campaigns/{slug}.md` | lock-on-write | Only the owning Archon instance updates this file. Fleet agents that produce campaign-adjacent work report it in their HANDOFF and let Archon update. |
 308: | `.planning/telemetry/*.jsonl` | append-only | Append-only log. Multiple agents may append simultaneously — JSONL format guarantees each line is an atomic write. |
 309: | `.planning/wiki/_staging/*.jsonl` | append-only | Each agent writes a uniquely-named staging file. Compile step resolves all of them in order. No concurrent write conflicts possible. |
 310: | `.planning/coordination/claims/*.json` | lock-on-write | Each agent owns its own claim file (named by instance ID). No sharing; no conflicts. |
 311: 
 312: **Enforcement:** Before Step 3 (spawn agents), remind each agent in its context
 313: injection which paths it may write to and what the strategy is for each. Agents
 314: that attempt to modify lock-on-write resources they don't own must be blocked via
 315: scope claim verification.
 316: 
 317: ## Consistency Voting (High-Stakes Decisions)
 318: 
 319: For decisions that cannot easily be undone — campaign completion approval, campaign
 320: merge to main, fleet abort — spawn 3 judgment agents in parallel and require 2/3
 321: agreement before proceeding.
 322: 
 323: **When to vote:**
 324: - Proposing to mark a multi-wave fleet `completed` when any wave is `partial`
 325: - Merging an agent's branch when its phase validator returned a `fail` (even after retries)
 326: - Deciding to abort and discard work from a wave with mixed results
 327: 
 328: **How to vote:**
 329: ```
 330: Vote prompt: "Fleet session {slug}, Wave {N}. The proposed decision is: {decision}.
 331:              Evidence: {handoff summaries, validator results}. 
 332:              Should we proceed? Respond with JSON: {verdict: 'proceed'|'block', reason: '...'}"
 333: ```
 334: 
 335: Spawn 3 Phase Validators with the vote prompt. Collect results. Tally:
 336: - 3/3 proceed → proceed
 337: - 2/3 proceed → proceed, log the dissenting reason
 338: - 2/3 block → block, escalate to user with the reasons
 339: - 3/3 block → block
 340: 
 341: If voting agents time out: count the timeout as a `proceed` vote (conservative — don't let validator failure park the fleet).
 342: 
 343: Skip voting when the decision is clearly safe (all waves complete, all validators pass).
 344: 
 345: ## Coordination Safety
 346: 
 347: ### Instance ID Generation
 348: 
 349: Every agent spawned by Fleet must have a unique instance ID.
 350: 
 351: Format: `fleet-{session-slug}-{wave}-{agent-index}`
 352: Example: `fleet-auth-refactor-w1-a3` (wave 1, agent 3)
 353: 
 354: The instance ID is:
 355: - Written to the agent's worktree as `.fleet-instance-id`
 356: - Included in all telemetry log entries for this agent
 357: - Used in coordination claims to identify which agent owns which scope
 358: - Used in dead instance recovery to identify orphaned claims
 359: 
 360: ### Scope Overlap Detection
 361: 
 362: Before spawning: compare all agent scopes pairwise (directory scopes overlap any file inside them). On overlap: merge tasks, narrow scopes, or sequence. **NEVER proceed with overlapping scopes.**
 363: 
 364: ### Dead Instance Recovery
 365: 
 366: After each wave: read `.planning/coordination/claims/`, verify each instance is still alive (worktree exists + HANDOFF present). Release orphaned claims, return uncompleted scope to next wave's queue.
 367: 
 368: ## Fringe Cases
 369: 
 370: - **`.planning/fleet/` does not exist**: Create the directory before writing the session file.
 371: - **All agents in a wave fail**: Escalate to the user rather than proceeding to the next wave. Output which agents failed and why before continuing.
 372: - **Worktree checkout fails for an agent**: Skip that agent, log the failure in the session file, and continue. Record the skipped scope as a gap for the next wave.
 373: - **`.planning/` does not exist**: Create `.planning/fleet/` before starting. If `.planning/coordination/` is absent, skip scope claim registration.
 374: - **Discovery compression script missing**: Write raw HANDOFF excerpts to the briefs directory instead.
 375: - **Phase validator times out or returns malformed output**: Treat as pass with warning. Log and advance. Never block a wave on validator failure.
 376: - **All agents in a wave fail validation and exhaust retries**: Mark the entire wave `partial`. Log `wave_validator_halt`. Escalate to the user before proceeding to the next wave — partial wave results may invalidate downstream wave assumptions.
 377: 
 378: ## Speculative Mode
 379: 
 380: `/fleet --speculative N [direction]`
 381: 
 382: Try N different approaches to the same task simultaneously. Each approach gets its own
 383: worktree and branch. When all finish, the user picks the winner; losers are archived (not deleted).
 384: 
 385: ### Step 1: Decompose into N strategies
 386: 
 387: Before spawning, enumerate N distinct approaches. Each approach must:
 388: - Target the exact same files and end goal
 389: - Use a meaningfully different strategy (not just style variations)
 390: - Be feasible to complete in a single agent session
 391: 
 392: ### Step 2: Spawn N agents in parallel
 393: 
 394: Each agent gets:
 395: - The common direction (same for all)
 396: - Its strategy description (different per agent)
 397: - Its own branch name: `speculative/{session-slug}/{strategy-label}`
 398: - Instruction to set `branch` and `worktree_status: active` in its campaign frontmatter
 399: 
 400: Spawn with `isolation: "worktree"`. Scope overlap rules do NOT apply between speculative
 401: agents — they will all touch the same files intentionally.
 402: 
 403: ### Step 3: Collect and compare
 404: 
 405: After all agents complete, for each:
 406: 1. Read the HANDOFF
 407: 2. Run typecheck on that worktree's branch via `node scripts/run-with-timeout.js 300 <typecheck-cmd>`
 408: 3. Record in the session file: what was built, typecheck result, key decisions
 409: 
 410: Present a comparison table to the user:
 411: 
 412: | Strategy | Branch | Typecheck | Key Decision | Notable Tradeoffs |
 413: |----------|--------|-----------|--------------|-------------------|
 414: 
 415: If ALL N approaches fail typecheck: present the comparison table with all entries marked `FAIL typecheck`. Ask the user to pick the least-broken approach or abort. Do not proceed to Step 4 without a user decision.
 416: 
 417: ### Step 4: Archive losers, merge winner
 418: 
 419: When the user picks a winner:
 420: 1. **Winner**: Update campaign frontmatter `worktree_status: merged`, proceed with normal merge
 421: 2. **Losers**: Update campaign frontmatter `worktree_status: archived`. Do NOT delete branches.
 422: 
 423: ```bash
 424: # Optional: tag losers for clarity
 425: git tag archive/{loser-branch} {loser-branch}
 426: ```
 427: 
 428: Add `## Speculative Comparison` to session file: direction, N strategies, comparison table (strategy/branch/status/typecheck/notes), winner, merge timestamp.
 429: 
 430: ## Quick Mode
 431: 
 432: `/fleet --quick [task1]; [task2]; [task3]`
 433: 
 434: ### Differences from standard fleet
 435: 
 436: | Property | Standard Fleet | Quick Mode |
 437: |---|---|---|
 438: | Min streams | 3 | 2 |
 439: | Min complexity | 4 | 3 |
 440: | Waves | Multi-wave with discovery relay | Single wave only |
 441: | Session file | Written to `.planning/fleet/` | Skipped — results reported inline |
 442: | Discovery briefs | Compressed to `.planning/fleet/briefs/` | Skipped |
 443: | Merge | Per-wave confirmation | Auto-merge if no conflicts |
 444: | Scope claim | Written to coordination/ | Skipped |
 445: 
 446: ### Protocol
 447: 
 448: 1. Parse tasks from the `--quick` argument (semicolon-separated)
 449: 2. Validate scope overlap — if any two tasks touch the same files, merge them or sequence them
 450: 3. Spawn all agents simultaneously with `isolation: "worktree"`
 451: 4. Collect results; auto-merge worktrees if no conflicts detected
 452: 5. If merge conflict: surface to user, offer manual resolution
 453: 6. Report results inline — no session file written unless the user asks
 454: 
 455: ### When /do routes here
 456: 
 457: `/do` routes to `--quick` mode (not standard fleet) when:
 458: - Input contains "at the same time", "simultaneously", "in parallel", "both ... and"
 459: - Two or more clearly independent tasks are detected
 460: - Complexity is 3 (moderate), not 4+ (complex)
 461: - User chose "1" (yes once) or "2" (always) on the Fleet confirmation prompt
 462: 
 463: Entry from `/do` confirmation prompt: user chose yes (1) or always (2). Preferences stored under `consent.fleetSpawn` in harness.json via `readConsent`/`writeConsent`.
 464: 
 465: ## Contextual Gates
 466: 
 467: ### Disclosure
 468: - "Spawning {N} agents across {waves} waves in isolated worktrees. Estimated token budget: ~{tokens}K."
 469: - For speculative mode: "Running {N} parallel approaches to the same task. All will touch the same files."
 470: 
 471: ### Reversibility
 472: - **Green:** Single-wave fleet with < 3 agents
 473: - **Amber:** Multi-wave fleet (the default) -- each wave's merge is a separate commit
 474: - **Red:** Speculative mode or fleets that modify shared infrastructure
 475: 
 476: Red actions require explicit confirmation regardless of trust level.
 477: 
 478: ### Proportionality
 479: - **Standard fleet:** work queue requires 3+ independent streams. Fewer → downgrade to Marshal or Archon.
 480: - **Quick mode:** 2+ tasks with non-overlapping scopes. No minimum complexity gate.
 481: - If all streams touch the same directory: downgrade to sequential Archon phases
 482: - If estimated agents > 6: confirm with user (even trusted level)
 483: 
 484: ### Trust Gating
 485: Read trust level from `harness.json`:
 486: - **Novice** (0-4 sessions): Always confirm before spawning. Show agent count, scopes, and estimated cost.
 487: - **Familiar** (5-19 sessions): Confirm only for > 3 agents or speculative mode.
 488: - **Trusted** (20+ sessions): Auto-proceed for standard fleet. Confirm only for speculative mode or > 6 agents.
 489: 
 490: ## Exit Protocol
 491: 
 492: Update the session file, then output:
 493: 
 494: ```
 495: ---HANDOFF---
 496: - Fleet session: {name} — {waves completed} waves, {agents} agents total
 497: - Built: {summary of all wave results}
 498: - Discoveries: {key cross-agent findings}
 499: - Merge conflicts: {count and resolution}
 500: - Next: {remaining work if any}
 501: - Reversibility: amber -- multi-wave merges, revert each wave's merge commit
 502: ---
 503: ```

   1: #!/usr/bin/env node
   2: 
   3: /**
   4:  * external-action-gate.js - PreToolUse hook (Bash)
   5:  *
   6:  * Uses the First-Encounter Consent pattern to handle external actions.
   7:  *
   8:  * Three tiers:
   9:  *   SECRETS - Always blocked. Reading .env files via Bash.
  10:  *   HARD    - Always blocked per-action. Irreversible by default (merge, close,
  11:  *             delete, release, fork). Configurable via policy.externalActions.hard.
  12:  *   SOFT    - Governed by consent preference. Reversible by default (push, PR
  13:  *             create, comment). Configurable via policy.externalActions.soft.
  14:  *
  15:  * Policy overrides (harness.json):
  16:  *   policy.externalActions.protectedBranches - branches that can never be deleted
  17:  *   policy.externalActions.hard  - labels that are always per-action confirmed
  18:  *   policy.externalActions.soft  - labels governed by consent preference
  19:  *
  20:  * When a label appears in both hard[] and soft[], hard wins.
  21:  * When a label is in soft[] but was in default HARD, it moves to consent-gated.
  22:  * This lets users unlock merge/close for autonomous workflows.
  23:  *
  24:  * Exit codes:
  25:  *   0 = allowed
  26:  *   2 = blocked - message written to stderr so Claude Code surfaces it to the agent
  27:  */
  28:

  30: const {
  31:   detectExternalAction,
  32:   readExternalActionPolicy,
  33: } = require('../core/policy/external-actions');
  34: 
  35: const CITADEL_UI = process.env.CITADEL_UI === 'true';
  36:

  38: // For CLI: human-readable message to stderr — Claude Code includes stderr in the
  39: // hook error that the agent reads, so this is what surfaces as the block reason.
  40: function hookOutput(hookName, action, message, data = {}) {

  67: function run(input) {

  69:   try {
  70:     event = JSON.parse(input);
  71:   } catch {
  72:     process.exit(0);
  73:   }

  87:     hookOutput(
  88:       'external-action-gate',
  89:       'blocked',

  91:       `Reading .env files and credentials is always blocked.\n`,
  92:       { label: action.label, tier: action.tier }
  93:     );
  94:     process.exit(2);
  95:   }

  97:   if (action.kind === 'protected-branch') {
  98:     health.logBlock('external-action-gate', 'blocked', `delete protected branch ${action.branch}: ${command.slice(0, 200)}`);

 104:       `To unprotect it, remove it from policy.externalActions.protectedBranches.\n`,
 105:       { label: action.label, tier: action.tier }
 106:     );
 107:     process.exit(2);
 108:   }
 109: 
 110:   if (action.tier === 'allow') process.exit(0);
 111: 
 112:   if (action.tier === 'hard') {
 113:     health.logBlock('external-action-gate', 'blocked', `${action.label}: ${command.slice(0, 200)}`);
 114:     hookOutput(
 115:       'external-action-gate',

 118:       `Command: ${command.slice(0, 200)}\n\n` +
 119:       `This action cannot be undone. Please review the exact command above and explicitly\n` +
 120:       `confirm with the user before proceeding. Do not retry until confirmed.\n`,
 121:       { label: action.label, tier: action.tier }
 122:     );
 123:     process.exit(2);
 124:   }
 125: 
 126:   const consent = health.checkConsent('externalActions');
 127:   if (consent.action === 'allow') process.exit(0);
 128: 
 129:   if (consent.action === 'first-encounter') {
 130:     health.logBlock('external-action-gate', 'first-encounter', `${action.label}: ${command.slice(0, 200)}`);

 132:       'external-action-gate',

 168:     );
 169:   } else {
 170:     hookOutput(
 171:       'external-action-gate',
 172:       'consent-block',
 173:       `[Citadel] External action — approval required\n` +
 174:       `Action: ${action.label}  |  Command: ${command.slice(0, 120)}\n\n` +

 178:       `Then retry the command automatically.\n`,
 179:       { label: action.label, tier: action.tier, consent: 'always-ask' }

 166: /**
 167:  * Check whether the file being written falls within the active campaign's claimed scope.
 168:  * Warns (exit 0 with message) for out-of-scope writes.
 169:  * Hard-blocks (exit 2) only when the file appears in a "## Restricted Files" section.
 170:  *
 171:  * @param {string} relativePath - Path relative to project root, forward-slash separated
 172:  * @param {string} toolName - 'Edit' or 'Write'
 173:  * @param {string} _filePath - Absolute path (unused here but kept for signature clarity)
 174:  */
 175: function checkCampaignScope(relativePath, toolName, _filePath) {
 176:   try {
 177:     const campaign = findActiveCampaign(PROJECT_ROOT);
 178:     if (!campaign) return;
 179: 
 180:     const rawName = campaign.slug || 'campaign';
 181:     const campaignName = rawName.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9_\-]/g, '_');
 182: 
 183:     for (const entry of campaign.restrictedFiles || []) {
 184:       if (entry && matchPattern(relativePath, entry)) {
 185:         health.logBlock('protect-files', 'blocked-restricted', `${toolName} ${relativePath} (campaign: ${campaignName}, restricted: ${entry})`);
 186:         hookOutput('protect-files', 'blocked',
 187:           `[protect-files] Blocked: ${relativePath} is declared RESTRICTED by campaign "${campaignName}". ` +
 188:           `Remove it from the campaign's "Restricted Files" section to allow edits.`,
 189:           { file: relativePath, campaign: campaignName, restrictedEntry: entry, tool: toolName }
 190:         );
 191:         process.exit(2);
 192:       }
 193:     }
 194: 
 195:     const scopeEntries = campaign.claimedScope || [];
 196:     if (scopeEntries.length === 0) return;
 197: 
 198:     // Check if this file is within any claimed scope entry
 199:     for (const entry of scopeEntries) {
 200:       if (matchScopeEntry(relativePath, entry)) {
 201:         return; // Within claimed scope — allow silently
 202:       }
 203:     }
 204: 
 205:     // File is outside claimed scope — warn (advisory, not blocking)
 206:     const scopeList = scopeEntries.slice(0, 5).join(', ') + (scopeEntries.length > 5 ? '…' : '');
 207:     hookOutput('protect-files', 'warned',
 208:       `[protect-files] Warning: ${relativePath} is outside the claimed scope of campaign "${campaignName}". ` +
 209:       `Campaign scope: ${scopeList}. This is advisory — the write will proceed.`,
 210:       { file: relativePath, campaign: campaignName, scope: scopeEntries.slice(0, 5) }
 211:     );
 212:     health.increment('protect-files', 'scope-warning');
 213:   } catch {
 214:     // Any unexpected error — skip scope check silently (never block on check failure)
 215:   }
 216: }
 217: 
 218: /**
 219:  * Match a file path against a scope entry.
 220:  * Scope entries can be:
 221:  *   - A directory prefix (e.g., "src/auth/") → matches any file under it
 222:  *   - An exact file path (e.g., "src/auth/middleware.ts")
 223:  *   - A glob-like pattern (delegated to matchPattern)
 224:  *
 225:  * @param {string} filePath - Relative file path, forward-slash separated
 226:  * @param {string} entry - Scope entry from campaign file
 227:  * @returns {boolean}
 228:  */
 229: function matchScopeEntry(filePath, entry) {
 230:   // Normalize entry: treat bare directory names as prefix matches
 231:   if (!entry.includes('.') && !entry.endsWith('/')) {
 232:     // Looks like a directory without trailing slash — treat as prefix
 233:     if (filePath === entry || filePath.startsWith(entry + '/')) return true;
 234:   }
 235:   // Delegate to existing pattern matcher for /, /* patterns
 236:   return matchPattern(filePath, entry);
 237: }
 238: 
 239: /**
 240:  * Match a file path against a glob pattern.
 241:  *
 242:  * Supports:
 243:  *   - Exact path match
 244:  *   - dir/*  — files directly in a directory (single level)
 245:  *   - dir/   — all files under a directory prefix (trailing slash)
 246:  *   - src/** — recursive glob (any depth under src/)
 247:  *   - **\/*.ts — any .ts file at any depth
 248:  *
 249:  * @param {string} filePath - Relative file path, forward-slash separated
 250:  * @param {string} pattern - Glob pattern to match against
 251:  * @returns {boolean}
 252:  */
 253: function matchPattern(filePath, pattern) {
 254:   // Exact match
 255:   if (filePath === pattern) return true;
 256: 
 257:   // Recursive glob: pattern contains **
 258:   if (pattern.includes('**')) {
 259:     return matchGlobStar(filePath, pattern);
 260:   }
 261: 
 262:   // Single-level wildcard: pattern ends with /*
 263:   if (pattern.endsWith('/*')) {
 264:     const dir = pattern.slice(0, -2);
 265:     return filePath.startsWith(dir + '/') && !filePath.slice(dir.length + 1).includes('/');
 266:   }
 267: 
 268:   // Directory prefix: pattern ends with /
 269:   if (pattern.endsWith('/')) {
 270:     return filePath.startsWith(pattern);
 271:   }
 272: 
 273:   return false;
 274: }
 275: 
 276: /**
 277:  * Match a file path against a glob pattern containing **.
 278:  * ** matches any sequence of path segments (including zero).
 279:  * Examples:
 280:  *   src/** matches src/foo.ts, src/a/b/c.ts
 281:  *   **\/*.ts matches any .ts file at any depth
 282:  *   src/**\/index.ts matches src/foo/index.ts, src/foo/bar/index.ts
 283:  *
 284:  * @param {string} filePath - Relative file path, forward-slash separated
 285:  * @param {string} pattern - Glob pattern containing **
 286:  * @returns {boolean}
 287:  */
 288: function matchGlobStar(filePath, pattern) {
 289:   // Convert glob to regex
 290:   // Escape regex special chars except * and /
 291:   const escaped = pattern
 292:     .replace(/[.+^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')  // escape regex specials
 293:     .replace(/\*\*/g, '\x00')               // temporarily replace ** with placeholder
 294:     .replace(/\*/g, '[^/]*')                // * → match non-slash chars
 295:     .replace(/\x00/g, '.*');                // ** → match anything (including slashes)
 296: 
 297:   try {
 298:     const re = new RegExp('^' + escaped + '$');
 299:     return re.test(filePath);
 300:   } catch {
 301:     return false; // malformed pattern — fail safe (allow the action)
 302:   }
 303: }
 304: 
 305: main();

   2: 
   3: /**
   4:  * worktree-setup.js — WorktreeCreate hook
   5:  *
   6:  * Auto-initializes new git worktrees for parallel agent execution.
   7:  * Runs package install and copies environment files so sub-agents
   8:  * can run typecheck, tests, and builds immediately.
   9:  *
  10:  * Receives stdin JSON: { "name": "agent-abc123", "path": "/path/to/worktree" }
  11:  *
  12:  * Exit codes:
  13:  *   0 = setup complete (or skipped gracefully)
  14:  *   2 = setup failed (blocks worktree creation)
  15:  */
  16: 
  17: const { execFileSync } = require('child_process');
  18: const path = require('path');
  19: const fs = require('fs');
  20: const health = require('./harness-health-util');
  21: 
  22: const MAIN_ROOT = health.PROJECT_ROOT;
  23: 
  24: function main(input) {
  25:   const worktreePath = input.path;
  26:   if (!worktreePath) return;
  27: 
  28:   const pathCheck = health.validatePath(worktreePath);
  29:   if (!pathCheck.safe) {
  30:     health.securityWarning('worktree-setup', `Possible injection in worktree path — ${pathCheck.violation}. Skipping setup.`);
  31:     return;
  32:   }
  33: 
  34:   // Verify the worktree has a package.json (Node project)
  35:   if (fs.existsSync(path.join(worktreePath, 'package.json'))) {
  36:     // Skip if node_modules already exists (resuming a worktree)
  37:     if (!fs.existsSync(path.join(worktreePath, 'node_modules'))) {
  38:       const config = health.readConfig();
  39:       const pm = config.packageManager || 'npm';
  40:       const [cmd, args] = pm === 'pnpm' ? ['pnpm', ['install', '--frozen-lockfile']]
  41:         : pm === 'yarn' ? ['yarn', ['install', '--frozen-lockfile']]
  42:         : pm === 'bun' ? ['bun', ['install', '--frozen-lockfile']]
  43:         : ['npm', ['ci', '--prefer-offline']];
  44: 
  45:       try {
  46:         execFileSync(cmd, args, {
  47:           cwd: worktreePath,
  48:           timeout: 120000,
  49:           encoding: 'utf8',
  50:           stdio: ['pipe', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
  51:         });
  52:       } catch (err) {
  53:         process.stderr.write(`[worktree-setup] Install failed in ${worktreePath}: ${err.message}\n`);
  54:         // Don't block worktree creation — typecheck will gracefully skip
  55:       }
  56:     }
  57:   }
  58: 
  59:   // For Python: create venv if requirements.txt exists
  60:   // Security: only run pip install if the requirements.txt is tracked by git
  61:   // (committed to the repository), not if it was written solely by an agent
  62:   // during this session. This prevents supply-chain attacks via agent-generated
  63:   // package lists. Set CITADEL_ALLOW_UNTRACKED_PIP=true to opt-out (e.g. greenfield).
  64:   if (fs.existsSync(path.join(worktreePath, 'requirements.txt'))) {

   3: /**
   4:  * Circuit Breaker — PostToolUseFailure + PostToolUse Hook
   5:  *
   6:  * Two modes:
   7:  * 1. PostToolUseFailure: tracks consecutive tool failures. After 3 failures,
   8:  *    injects a message suggesting a different approach. After 5 trips in a
   9:  *    session, escalates to a hard "stop and rethink" message.
  10:  * 2. PostToolUse (Bash only): checks last-command-result.json for commands
  11:  *    that exceeded the hang threshold (default 300s from harness.json
  12:  *    agentTimeouts.command). Treats long-running commands as soft failures.
  13:  *
  14:  * State file: .claude/circuit-breaker-state.json
  15:  * Counter resets when the threshold is hit (self-contained state).

  27:       hook: hookName,
  28:       action,
  29:       message,

  34:     process.stdout.write(message);

  45: function getHangThreshold() {
  46:   try {
  47:     const harnessPath = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.claude', 'harness.json');
  48:     if (fs.existsSync(harnessPath)) {
  49:       const harness = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(harnessPath, 'utf8'));
  50:       const t = harness?.agentTimeouts?.command;
  51:       if (typeof t === 'number' && t > 0) return t;
  52:     }
  53:   } catch { /* fall through */ }
  54:   return 300; // default: 5 minutes
  55: }
  56: 
  57: function readState() {
  58:   // Try PLUGIN_DATA_DIR first, fall back to legacy .claude/ location
  59:   const stateFile = fs.existsSync(STATE_FILE) ? STATE_FILE
  60:     : fs.existsSync(LEGACY_STATE_FILE) ? LEGACY_STATE_FILE
  61:     : null;

  63:     if (stateFile) return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(stateFile, 'utf8'));

  66: }
  67: 
  68: function writeState(state) {
  69:   const dir = path.dirname(STATE_FILE);
  70:   if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  71:   const tmpFile = STATE_FILE + '.tmp';
  72:   fs.writeFileSync(tmpFile, JSON.stringify(state, null, 2));
  73:   fs.renameSync(tmpFile, STATE_FILE);
  74: }
