  76: /**
  77:  * Handle PostToolUseFailure: track consecutive failures, trip on threshold.
  78:  */
  79: function handleFailure(event) {
  80:   const toolName = event.tool_name || 'unknown';
  81:   const error = (event.error || event.tool_error || '').toString().slice(0, 200);
  82: 
  83:   health.increment('circuit-breaker', 'count');
  84: 
  85:   const state = readState();
  86:   state.consecutiveFailures += 1;
  87:   state.lastFailedTool = toolName;
  88:   state.lastFailureTime = new Date().toISOString();
  89:   state.lastError = error || null;
  90: 
  91:   writeState(state);
  92: 
  93:   if (state.consecutiveFailures >= THRESHOLD) {
  94:     health.increment('circuit-breaker', 'trips');
  95:     const lifetimeTrips = (state.lifetimeTrips || 0) + 1;
  96:     // Reset consecutive counter, preserve lifetime trips
  97:     writeState({
  98:       consecutiveFailures: 0,
  99:       lifetimeTrips,
 100:       lastFailedTool: null,
 101:       lastFailureTime: null,
 102:       lastError: null,
 103:     });
 104: 
 105:     const lines = [
 106:       `[Circuit Breaker] The "${toolName}" tool has failed ${THRESHOLD} times in a row (trip #${lifetimeTrips} this session).`,
 107:       error ? `  Error: ${error}` : null,
 108:       `  What this means: Citadel detected a repeated failure pattern and is suggesting you change approach.`,
 109:     ];
 110: 
 111:     if (lifetimeTrips >= 5) {
 112:       lines.push(
 113:         ``,
 114:         `  WARNING: ${lifetimeTrips} trips this session. You are stuck in a failure loop.`,
 115:         `  STOP trying variations of the same approach. Step back and:`,
 116:         `  1. Re-read the relevant files from scratch — something may have changed`,
 117:         `  2. Consider whether the approach is fundamentally wrong`,
 118:         `  3. Try a completely different strategy, not a minor variation`,
 119:         `  4. If stuck: describe the problem to the user and ask for guidance`,
 120:       );
 121:     } else {
 122:       lines.push(
 123:         ``,
 124:         `  Try a different approach:`,
 125:         `  - "${toolName}" + Edit/Write failing? Re-read the file first — content may have changed`,
 126:         `  - "${toolName}" + Bash failing? Check if a prerequisite step was missed`,
 127:         `  - "${toolName}" + Grep/Glob failing? Try broader patterns or different paths`,
 128:         `  - Same tool keeps failing? Switch to an alternative tool or approach`,
 129:       );
 130:     }
 131: 
 132:     const msg = lines.filter(Boolean).join('\n');
 133:     hookOutput('circuit-breaker', 'warned', msg, {
 134:       consecutiveFailures: THRESHOLD,
 135:       lifetimeTrips,
 136:       lastFailedTool: toolName,
 137:       lastError: error || null,
 138:     });
 139:   }
 140: }

 142: /**
 143:  * Handle PostToolUse for Bash: check last-command-result.json for hangs.
 144:  * Only fires a warning -- does not trip the breaker or block execution.
 145:  */
 146: function handleBashHangCheck() {
 147:   try {
 148:     if (!fs.existsSync(LAST_CMD_RESULT)) return;
 149: 
 150:     const result = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(LAST_CMD_RESULT, 'utf8'));
 151: 
 152:     // Only check recent results (within last 10 seconds -- this run)
 153:     const resultAge = Date.now() - new Date(result.timestamp).getTime();
 154:     if (resultAge > 10000) return;
 155: 
 156:     const threshold = getHangThreshold();
 157:     const duration = result.durationSec || 0;
 158: 
 159:     if (result.timedOut) {
 160:       health.increment('circuit-breaker', 'hangs');
 161:       const state = readState();
 162:       state.consecutiveFailures += 1;
 163:       state.lastFailedTool = 'Bash (timeout)';
 164:       state.lastFailureTime = new Date().toISOString();
 165:       state.lastError = `Command killed after ${result.timeoutLimit}s: ${(result.command || '').slice(0, 100)}`;
 166:       writeState(state);
 167: 
 168:       hookOutput('circuit-breaker', 'warned',
 169:         `[Circuit Breaker] Command timed out after ${result.timeoutLimit}s and was killed.\n` +
 170:         `  Command: ${(result.command || '').slice(0, 150)}\n` +
 171:         `  This is a hard timeout. The command was terminated.\n` +
 172:         `  Consider: Is this command expected to take this long? If not, check for hangs.`,
 173:         { timedOut: true, command: result.command, duration: result.timeoutLimit }
 174:       );
 175:     } else if (duration > threshold) {
 176:       health.increment('circuit-breaker', 'hangs');
 177:       const state = readState();
 178:       state.consecutiveFailures += 1;
 179:       state.lastFailedTool = 'Bash (slow)';
 180:       state.lastFailureTime = new Date().toISOString();
 181:       state.lastError = `Command ran for ${duration}s (threshold: ${threshold}s): ${(result.command || '').slice(0, 100)}`;
 182:       writeState(state);
 183: 
 184:       hookOutput('circuit-breaker', 'warned',
 185:         `[Circuit Breaker] Command ran for ${duration}s (threshold: ${threshold}s). ` +
 186:         `This may indicate a hang. Consider adding a timeout to the command.\n` +
 187:         `  Command: ${(result.command || '').slice(0, 150)}\n` +
 188:         `  Use: node scripts/run-with-timeout.js ${threshold} <command>`,
 189:         { slow: true, command: result.command, duration, threshold }
 190:       );
 191:     }
 192:   } catch {
 193:     // Non-fatal -- don't break the hook pipeline over telemetry reads
 194:   }
 195: }

 198:   let input = '';
 199:   process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');
 200:   process.stdin.on('data', (chunk) => { input += chunk; });
 201:   process.stdin.on('end', () => {

**Identifying potential issues**

I see a concerning issue: the worktree is copying `.env` and `.env.local` files, which could inadvertently expose secrets. That's something I definitely need to note. Additionally, the worktree validate function only checks the path without confirming if it remains within valid worktrees. I may need to consider using `health.validatePath`. It could also be beneficial to fetch `harness-health-util` for additional checks like `validatePath` and `readConfig`. Plus, I should look into the external policy at `core/policy/external-actions`.
codex
The hooks are substantive, not just manifest claims: there is consent gating, path validation, protected file checks, and a circuit breaker. One security concern is also visible in code: the worktree setup copies `.env`/`.env.local` into agent worktrees, so I’m checking the shared utility and policy detector before scoring security.

   3: /**
   4:  * harness-health-util.js — Shared utilities for harness hooks.
   5:  *
   6:  * Provides lightweight telemetry and health tracking used by other hooks.
   7:  * All state is file-based (JSON/JSONL) — no databases, no services.

  10: const fs = require('fs');
  11: const path = require('path');
  12: const crypto = require('crypto');
  13: 
  14: const PROJECT_ROOT = process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd();
  15: const TELEMETRY_DIR = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.planning', 'telemetry');
  16: const HOOK_TIMING_FILE = path.join(TELEMETRY_DIR, 'hook-timing.jsonl');
  17: const AUDIT_LOG_FILE = path.join(TELEMETRY_DIR, 'audit.jsonl');
  18: 
  19: // Debug mode — set CITADEL_DEBUG=true in .claude/settings.json env to enable.
  20: // Prints a one-line summary to stderr each time a hook fires or completes.
  21: // Follows the same opt-in pattern as CITADEL_UI.
  22: const CITADEL_DEBUG = process.env.CITADEL_DEBUG === 'true';
  23: 
  24: // ── Audit integrity ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  25: 
  26: /**
  27:  * Recursively sort object keys for deterministic JSON serialization.
  28:  * Arrays are preserved in order; object keys are sorted alphabetically.
  29:  * Primitive values are returned as-is.
  30:  */
  31: function canonicalize(obj) {
  32:   if (Array.isArray(obj)) return obj.map(canonicalize);
  33:   if (obj !== null && typeof obj === 'object') {
  34:     const sorted = {};
  35:     for (const key of Object.keys(obj).sort()) {
  36:       sorted[key] = canonicalize(obj[key]);
  37:     }
  38:     return sorted;
  39:   }
  40:   return obj;
  41: }
  42: 
  43: /**
  44:  * Compute a SHA-256 hash of a telemetry record.
  45:  * The record must NOT contain _hash or _hash_v fields — those are added after.
  46:  * @param {object} record - Plain object without hash fields
  47:  * @returns {string} Hex-encoded SHA-256 digest
  48:  */
  49: function hashRecord(record) {
  50:   const canonical = JSON.stringify(canonicalize(record));
  51:   return crypto.createHash('sha256').update(canonical, 'utf8').digest('hex');
  52: }
  53: 
  54: /**
  55:  * Verify the integrity of a telemetry JSONL file.
  56:  * Checks each record's _hash field by recomputing the hash from the record body.
  57:  *
  58:  * @param {string} [file] - Path to the JSONL file (defaults to audit.jsonl)
  59:  * @returns {{ total: number, verified: number, tampered: object[], legacy: string[] }}
  60:  *   total     — number of parseable records
  61:  *   verified  — records whose hash matched
  62:  *   tampered  — records whose hash did not match (possible corruption or tampering)
  63:  *   legacy    — records written before hashing was added (no _hash field)
  64:  */
  65: function verifyAuditIntegrity(file) {
  66:   const targetFile = file || AUDIT_LOG_FILE;
  67:   const results = { total: 0, verified: 0, tampered: [], legacy: [] };
  68:   try {
  69:     if (!fs.existsSync(targetFile)) return results;
  70:     const lines = fs.readFileSync(targetFile, 'utf8').split('\n').filter(Boolean);
  71:     for (const line of lines) {
  72:       let record;
  73:       try { record = JSON.parse(line); } catch { continue; }
  74:       results.total++;
  75: 
  76:       if (!record._hash || record._hash_v !== 1) {
  77:         results.legacy.push(record.timestamp || 'unknown-timestamp');
  78:         continue;
  79:       }
  80: 
  81:       // Reconstruct the pre-hash body and recompute
  82:       const { _hash, _hash_v, ...body } = record; // eslint-disable-line no-unused-vars
  83:       const expected = hashRecord(body);
  84:       if (expected === _hash) {
  85:         results.verified++;
  86:       } else {
  87:         results.tampered.push({
  88:           timestamp: record.timestamp,
  89:           event: record.event || record.hook,
  90:           expected,
  91:           got: _hash,
  92:         });
  93:       }
  94:     }
  95:   } catch { /* non-critical — always return a result */ }
  96:   return results;
  97: }
  98: 
  99: /**
 100:  * Emit a debug line to stderr when CITADEL_DEBUG=true.
 101:  * Uses stderr (not stdout) so it doesn't interfere with hook exit codes or
 102:  * structured JSON output on stdout.
 103:  *
 104:  * @param {string} hook  - Hook file name (e.g. 'post-edit', 'circuit-breaker')

 106:  * @param {string} [detail] - Optional one-line context (file path, rule name, etc.)

 223:     docs: {
 224:       auto: true,                 // false to disable automatic doc sync
 225:       audiences: ['user', 'org', 'agents'],
 226:       exclude: [],
 227:     },
 228:     trust: {
 229:       sessions_completed: 0,
 230:       campaigns_completed: 0,
 231:       campaigns_reverted: 0,
 232:       fleet_clean_merges: 0,
 233:       improve_loops_accepted: 0,
 234:       daemon_runs: 0,
 235:       override: null,
 236:     },
 237:   };
 238: }

 240: /**
 241:  * Detect the project language from files in the project root.
 242:  * @returns {{ language: string, framework: string|null, packageManager: string }}
 243:  */
 244: function detectStack() {
 245:   const exists = (f) => fs.existsSync(path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, f));
 246: 
 247:   let language = 'unknown';
 248:   let framework = null;
 249:   let packageManager = 'npm';
 250: 
 251:   // Language detection
 252:   if (exists('tsconfig.json') || exists('tsconfig.app.json')) {
 253:     language = 'typescript';
 254:   } else if (exists('package.json')) {
 255:     language = 'javascript';
 256:   } else if (exists('requirements.txt') || exists('pyproject.toml') || exists('setup.py')) {
 257:     language = 'python';
 258:   } else if (exists('go.mod')) {
 259:     language = 'go';
 260:   } else if (exists('Cargo.toml')) {
 261:     language = 'rust';
 262:   } else if (exists('build.gradle') || exists('pom.xml')) {
 263:     language = 'java';
 264:   }
 265: 
 266:   // Framework detection (language-specific)
 267:   if (language === 'typescript' || language === 'javascript') {
 268:     try {
 269:       const pkg = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
 270:       const allDeps = { ...pkg.dependencies, ...pkg.devDependencies };
 271:       if (allDeps['react']) framework = 'react';
 272:       else if (allDeps['vue']) framework = 'vue';
 273:       else if (allDeps['svelte']) framework = 'svelte';
 274:       else if (allDeps['@angular/core']) framework = 'angular';
 275:       else if (allDeps['express'] || allDeps['fastify'] || allDeps['hono']) framework = 'node-api';
 276:       else if (allDeps['next']) framework = 'nextjs';
 277:     } catch { /* no package.json readable */ }
 278:   } else if (language === 'python') {
 279:     if (exists('manage.py')) framework = 'django';
 280:     else if (exists('app.py') || exists('wsgi.py')) framework = 'flask';
 281:     else if (exists('main.py')) framework = 'fastapi';
 282:   }
 283: 
 284:   // Package manager detection
 285:   if (exists('pnpm-lock.yaml')) packageManager = 'pnpm';
 286:   else if (exists('yarn.lock')) packageManager = 'yarn';
 287:   else if (exists('bun.lockb')) packageManager = 'bun';
 288: 
 289:   return { language, framework, packageManager };
 290: }
 291: 
 292: /**
 293:  * Get the typecheck command for a given language.
 294:  * @param {string} language
 295:  * @param {boolean} perFile - Whether to check a single file
 296:  * @returns {{ command: string|null, perFile: boolean }}
 297:  */
 298: function getTypecheckConfig(language) {
 299:   switch (language) {
 300:     case 'typescript':
 301:       return { command: 'npx tsc --noEmit', perFile: true };
 302:     case 'python':
 303:       // Try mypy first, fall back to pyright
 304:       try {
 305:         require('child_process').execFileSync('mypy', ['--version'], { stdio: 'pipe' });
 306:         return { command: 'mypy', perFile: true };
 307:       } catch {
 308:         try {
 309:           require('child_process').execFileSync('pyright', ['--version'], { stdio: 'pipe' });
 310:           return { command: 'pyright', perFile: true };
 311:         } catch {
 312:           return { command: null, perFile: false };
 313:         }
 314:       }
 315:     case 'go':
 316:       return { command: 'go vet', perFile: false };
 317:     case 'rust':
 318:       return { command: 'cargo check', perFile: false };
 319:     case 'java':
 320:       return { command: null, perFile: false };
 321:     default:
 322:       return { command: null, perFile: false };
 323:   }
 324: }

 327: /**
 328:  * Log a hook block or error event to hook-errors.jsonl.
 329:  * @param {string} hook - Hook name
 330:  * @param {string} action - What happened ('blocked', 'error', 'parse-fail')
 331:  * @param {string} detail - What was blocked or what failed
 332:  */
 333: function logBlock(hook, action, detail) {
 334:   ensureTelemetryDir();
 335:   try {
 336:     const entry = JSON.stringify({
 337:       schema: 1,
 338:       timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
 339:       hook,
 340:       action,
 341:       detail,
 342:     });
 343:     fs.appendFileSync(path.join(TELEMETRY_DIR, 'hook-errors.jsonl'), entry + '\n', 'utf8');
 344:   } catch { /* telemetry should never break the hook */ }
 345: }
 346: 
 347: /**
 348:  * Compute the trust level from harness config counters.
 349:  * @returns {{ level: 'novice'|'familiar'|'trusted', trust: object }}
 350:  */
 351: function readTrustLevel() {
 352:   const config = readConfig();
 353:   const defaults = {
 354:     sessions_completed: 0,
 355:     campaigns_completed: 0,
 356:     campaigns_reverted: 0,
 357:     fleet_clean_merges: 0,
 358:     improve_loops_accepted: 0,
 359:     daemon_runs: 0,
 360:     override: null,
 361:   };
 362:   const trust = { ...defaults, ...(config.trust || {}) };
 363: 
 364:   // Explicit override takes priority
 365:   const validOverrides = ['novice', 'familiar', 'trusted'];
 366:   if (trust.override && validOverrides.includes(trust.override)) {
 367:     return { level: trust.override, trust };
 368:   }
 369: 
 370:   // Compute from counters
 371:   let level = 'novice';
 372:   if (trust.sessions_completed >= 20 && trust.campaigns_completed >= 2) {
 373:     level = 'trusted';
 374:   } else if (trust.sessions_completed >= 5) {
 375:     level = 'familiar';
 376:   }
 377: 
 378:   return { level, trust };
 379: }
 380: 
 381: // ── Input Validation ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 382: 
 383: // Paths allow backslash (Windows path separator).
 384: // Commands reject backslash — expected to be simple tool names (e.g., "mypy"),
 385: // not absolute paths. Windows users with paths like C:\Python39\python.exe
 386: // would need to add the tool to PATH instead.
 387: const PATH_META_RE = /[`$;|&\n\r\0]|\$\(/;
 388: const CMD_META_RE = /[`$;|&\n\r\0\\]|\$\(/;
 389: // Detect path traversal sequences: ../ or ..\ (both forward and back slash)
 390: const PATH_TRAVERSAL_RE = /\.\.[/\\]/;
 391: 
 392: /** @returns {{ safe: boolean, violation: string|null }} */
 393: function _validateInput(value, label, regex) {
 394:   if (!value || typeof value !== 'string') {
 395:     return { safe: false, violation: `empty or non-string ${label}` };
 396:   }
 397:   const match = value.match(regex);
 398:   if (match) {
 399:     return {
 400:       safe: false,
 401:       violation: `shell metacharacter ${JSON.stringify(match[0])} in ${label}: ${value.slice(0, 200)}`,
 402:     };
 403:   }
 404:   // For paths: also reject traversal sequences (../  or ..\)
 405:   if (label === 'path' && PATH_TRAVERSAL_RE.test(value)) {
 406:     return {
 407:       safe: false,
 408:       violation: `path traversal sequence in ${label}: ${value.slice(0, 200)}`,
 409:     };
 410:   }
 411:   return { safe: true, violation: null };
 412: }
 413: 
 414: function validatePath(filePath) { return _validateInput(filePath, 'path', PATH_META_RE); }
 415: function validateCommand(command) { return _validateInput(command, 'command', CMD_META_RE); }
 416: 
 417: function securityWarning(hook, message) {
 418:   const msg = `[SECURITY] ${hook}: ${message}\n`;
 419:   if (process.env.CITADEL_UI === 'true') {
 420:     process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({
 421:       hook,
 422:       action: 'security-warning',
 423:       message: msg.trim(),
 424:       timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
 425:       data: {},
 426:     }));
 427:   } else {
 428:     process.stdout.write(msg);
 429:   }
 430:   increment(hook, 'security-warning');
 431: }
 432: 
 433: /**
 434:  * Append an entry to the immutable audit log.
 435:  * The audit log is append-only — never truncated, never overwritten.
 436:  * Records significant agent actions, policy violations, and system events.
 437:  *
 438:  * @param {string} event - Event type (e.g., 'scope-violation', 'subagent-stop', 'worktree-removed')
 439:  * @param {object} data - Structured data for the event
 440:  */
 441: function writeAuditLog(event, data = {}) {
 442:   ensureTelemetryDir();
 443:   try {
 444:     const base = {
 445:       schema: 1,
 446:       event,
 447:       timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
 448:       project: path.basename(PROJECT_ROOT),
 449:       ...data,
 450:     };
 451:     const entry = JSON.stringify({ ...base, _hash: hashRecord(base), _hash_v: 1 });
 452:     fs.appendFileSync(AUDIT_LOG_FILE, entry + '\n', 'utf8');
 453:   } catch { /* audit log should never break a hook */ }
 454: }
 455: 
 456: // ── Consent System ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 457: 
 458: /**
 459:  * First-Encounter Consent pattern.
 460:  *
 461:  * On the first occurrence of a protected action category, the hook pauses
 462:  * and informs the user what's about to happen. The user picks one of:
 463:  *   - "always-ask"        Block every time, require explicit approval
 464:  *   - "session-allow"     Allow for this session, ask fresh next session
 465:  *   - "auto-allow"        Trust the agent, never ask again for this category
 466:  *
 467:  * Preferences are stored in harness.json under "consent".
 468:  * Session-scoped allows use a timestamp file in PLUGIN_DATA_DIR.
 469:  *
 470:  * Categories:
 471:  *   - externalActions     git push, gh pr create/merge, gh issue comment, etc.
 472:  *   - daemonSpend         Autonomous multi-session spending
 473:  *   - fleetSpawn          Parallel agent spawning (cost multiplication)
 474:  */
 475: 
 476: const CONSENT_CATEGORIES = ['externalActions', 'daemonSpend', 'fleetSpawn'];
 477: 
 478: /**
 479:  * Read the consent preference for a category.
 480:  * @param {string} category - One of CONSENT_CATEGORIES
 481:  * @returns {'always-ask'|'session-allow'|'auto-allow'|null} null = first encounter
 482:  */
 483: function readConsent(category) {
 484:   const config = readConfig();
 485:   const consent = config.consent || {};
 486:   const pref = consent[category] || null;
 487:   if (!pref) return null;
 488:   if (!['always-ask', 'session-allow', 'auto-allow'].includes(pref)) return null;
 489:   return pref;
 490: }
 491: 
 492: /**
 493:  * Write a consent preference for a category to harness.json.
 494:  * Creates harness.json if it doesn't exist.
 495:  * @param {string} category
 496:  * @param {'always-ask'|'session-allow'|'auto-allow'} preference
 497:  */
 498: function writeConsent(category, preference) {
 499:   const configPath = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.claude', 'harness.json');
 500:   let config = {};
 501:   try {
 502:     if (fs.existsSync(configPath)) {
 503:       config = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(configPath, 'utf8'));
 504:     }
 505:   } catch { /* start fresh */ }
 506: 
 507:   if (!config.consent) config.consent = {};
 508:   config.consent[category] = preference;
 509: 
 510:   const dir = path.dirname(configPath);
 511:   if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
 512:   fs.writeFileSync(configPath, JSON.stringify(config, null, 2));
 513: }
 514: 
 515: /**
 516:  * Check if a session-scoped allow is active for a category.
 517:  * Session allows expire after 6 hours (covers long sessions but not overnight).
 518:  * @param {string} category
 519:  * @returns {boolean}
 520:  */
 521: function hasSessionAllow(category) {
 522:   const markerPath = path.join(PLUGIN_DATA_DIR, `consent-session-${category}.json`);
 523:   try {
 524:     if (!fs.existsSync(markerPath)) return false;
 525:     const marker = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(markerPath, 'utf8'));
 526:     const age = Date.now() - new Date(marker.timestamp).getTime();
 527:     const SIX_HOURS = 6 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
 528:     return age < SIX_HOURS;
 529:   } catch { return false; }
 530: }
 531: 
 532: /**
 533:  * Grant a session-scoped allow for a category.
 534:  * @param {string} category
 535:  */
 536: function grantSessionAllow(category) {
 537:   const markerPath = path.join(PLUGIN_DATA_DIR, `consent-session-${category}.json`);
 538:   const dir = path.dirname(markerPath);
 539:   if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
 540:   fs.writeFileSync(markerPath, JSON.stringify({ category, timestamp: new Date().toISOString() }));
 541: }
 542: 
 543: /**
 544:  * Grant a one-time approval for a specific action.
 545:  * Consumed on read (single-use). Expires after 120 seconds.
 546:  * Used by "always-ask" consent: hook blocks, user approves, Claude writes
 547:  * a one-time marker, retries, hook consumes the marker and allows.
 548:  * @param {string} category
 549:  */
 550: function grantOneTimeAllow(category) {
 551:   const markerPath = path.join(PLUGIN_DATA_DIR, `consent-onetime-${category}.json`);
 552:   const dir = path.dirname(markerPath);
 553:   if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
 554:   fs.writeFileSync(markerPath, JSON.stringify({ category, timestamp: new Date().toISOString() }));
 555: }
 556: 
 557: /**
 558:  * Check and consume a one-time approval. Returns true if a fresh marker exists.
 559:  * The marker is deleted after reading (single-use).
 560:  * @param {string} category
 561:  * @returns {boolean}
 562:  */
 563: function consumeOneTimeAllow(category) {
 564:   const markerPath = path.join(PLUGIN_DATA_DIR, `consent-onetime-${category}.json`);
 565:   try {
 566:     if (!fs.existsSync(markerPath)) return false;
 567:     const marker = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(markerPath, 'utf8'));
 568:     // Always consume (delete) the marker
 569:     fs.unlinkSync(markerPath);
 570:     const age = Date.now() - new Date(marker.timestamp).getTime();
 571:     const TWO_MINUTES = 120 * 1000;
 572:     return age < TWO_MINUTES;
 573:   } catch {
 574:     try { fs.unlinkSync(markerPath); } catch { /* already gone */ }
 575:     return false;
 576:   }
 577: }
 578: 
 579: /**
 580:  * Determine if a protected action should proceed based on consent.
 581:  *
 582:  * Returns:
 583:  *   - { action: 'allow' }           Proceed silently
 584:  *   - { action: 'first-encounter' } No preference set yet, hook should inform and block
 585:  *   - { action: 'block' }           User chose always-ask, hook should block
 586:  *
 587:  * @param {string} category
 588:  * @returns {{ action: 'allow'|'block'|'first-encounter' }}
 589:  */
 590: function checkConsent(category) {
 591:   const pref = readConsent(category);
 592: 
 593:   // First encounter -- no preference stored yet
 594:   if (!pref) return { action: 'first-encounter' };
 595: 
 596:   // Auto-allow -- user trusts the agent for this category
 597:   if (pref === 'auto-allow') return { action: 'allow' };
 598: 
 599:   // Session-allow -- check if current session has been granted
 600:   if (pref === 'session-allow') {
 601:     if (hasSessionAllow(category)) return { action: 'allow' };
 602:     return { action: 'block' };
 603:   }
 604: 
 605:   // Always-ask -- check for one-time approval (user approved in conversation)
 606:   if (pref === 'always-ask') {
 607:     if (consumeOneTimeAllow(category)) return { action: 'allow' };
 608:     return { action: 'block' };
 609:   }
 610: 
 611:   return { action: 'block' };
 612: }
 613: 
 614: module.exports = {
 615:   increment,
 616:   logTiming,
 617:   logBlock,
 618:   debugLog,
 619:   writeAuditLog,
 620:   hashRecord,
 621:   canonicalize,
 622:   verifyAuditIntegrity,
 623:   readConfig,
 624:   readTrustLevel,
 625:   detectStack,
 626:   getTypecheckConfig,
 627:   validatePath,
 628:   validateCommand,
 629:   securityWarning,
 630:   readConsent,
 631:   writeConsent,
 632:   hasSessionAllow,
 633:   grantSessionAllow,
 634:   grantOneTimeAllow,
 635:   consumeOneTimeAllow,
 636:   checkConsent,
 637:   CONSENT_CATEGORIES,
 638:   PROJECT_ROOT,
 639:   TELEMETRY_DIR,
 640:   PLUGIN_DATA_DIR,
 641:   CITADEL_DEBUG,
 642: };

 succeeded in 479ms:
   1: 'use strict';
   2: 
   3: /**
   4:  * path-helpers.js — Cross-platform path normalization utilities.
   5:  *
   6:  * Two concerns live here:
   7:  *   1. Converting paths to forward-slash form for storage, logging, and comparison.
   8:  *   2. Detecting the shell environment (Git Bash vs native Windows vs Unix).
   9:  *
  10:  * IMPORTANT: toUniversal() is for storage/comparison ONLY.
  11:  * Do NOT pass its output to Node fs APIs — Node handles backslashes natively on
  12:  * Windows, and feeding it a forward-slash path from toUniversal can break in
  13:  * edge cases (UNC paths, drive-letter paths in POSIX mode).
  14:  */
  15: 
  16: const path = require('path');
  17: 
  18: /**
  19:  * Normalize a path to forward-slash form.
  20:  * Use when storing paths in JSON, JSONL, markdown tables, or log lines,
  21:  * and when comparing two paths as strings.
  22:  *
  23:  * @param {string} filePath
  24:  * @returns {string}
  25:  */
  26: function toUniversal(filePath) {
  27:   return String(filePath || '').split(path.sep).join('/');
  28: }
  29: 
  30: /**
  31:  * Normalize a path to the OS-native separator.
  32:  * Use when constructing paths for shell subprocesses or legacy tools that
  33:  * do not accept forward slashes on Windows.
  34:  *
  35:  * @param {string} filePath
  36:  * @returns {string}
  37:  */
  38: function toNative(filePath) {
  39:   return String(filePath || '').split(/[/\\]/).join(path.sep);
  40: }
  41: 
  42: /**
  43:  * Detect whether the current process is running inside Git Bash / MSYS2.
  44:  * Git Bash sets MSYSTEM to 'MINGW64', 'MINGW32', or 'MSYS'.
  45:  * Native Windows (cmd.exe, PowerShell) and WSL do not set it.
  46:  *
  47:  * @returns {boolean}
  48:  */
  49: function isGitBash() {
  50:   const msystem = process.env.MSYSTEM || '';
  51:   return msystem.startsWith('MINGW') || msystem === 'MSYS';
  52: }
  53: 
  54: /**
  55:  * Resolve the GitHub CLI executable path.
  56:  * Checks native Windows install locations first, then falls back to PATH lookup.
  57:  * Works correctly in Git Bash, native Windows, WSL, and Unix.
  58:  *
  59:  * @returns {string} Absolute path or 'gh' (relies on PATH)
  60:  */
  61: function resolveGhPath() {
  62:   if (process.platform === 'win32') {
  63:     const fs = require('fs');
  64:     const candidates = [
  65:       'C:\\Program Files\\GitHub CLI\\gh.exe',
  66:       'C:\\Program Files (x86)\\GitHub CLI\\gh.exe',
  67:     ];
  68:     for (const candidate of candidates) {
  69:       try {
  70:         if (fs.existsSync(candidate)) return candidate;
  71:       } catch {
  72:         // Non-critical — fall through to PATH
  73:       }
  74:     }
  75:   }
  76:   // Unix, WSL, Git Bash, or gh not in standard locations — rely on PATH
  77:   return 'gh';
  78: }
  79: 
  80: module.exports = {
  81:   toUniversal,
  82:   toNative,
  83:   isGitBash,
  84:   resolveGhPath,
  85: };

  33:   { regex: /\bgh\s+release\s+create\b/, label: 'gh release create' },
  34:   { regex: /gh\.exe"\s+release\s+create\b/, label: 'gh release create' },
  35:   { regex: /\bgh\s+repo\s+fork\b/, label: 'gh repo fork' },
  36:   { regex: /gh\.exe"\s+repo\s+fork\b/, label: 'gh repo fork' },
  37:   { regex: /\bgh\s+api\b.*--method\s+(POST|PUT|PATCH|DELETE)/i, label: 'gh api (mutating)' },
  38:   { regex: /gh\.exe"\s+api\b.*--method\s+(POST|PUT|PATCH|DELETE)/i, label: 'gh api (mutating)' },
  39: ];
  40: 
  41: const DEFAULT_HARD = [
  42:   'gh pr merge', 'gh pr close',
  43:   'gh issue close', 'gh issue delete',
  44:   'gh release create', 'gh repo fork',
  45:   'gh api (mutating)', 'git push --delete',
  46: ];
  47: 
  48: const DEFAULT_SOFT = [
  49:   'git push',
  50:   'gh pr create', 'gh pr comment/edit',
  51:   'gh issue create/comment/edit',
  52: ];
  53: 
  54: function readExternalActionPolicy(config) {
  55:   const externalActions = config?.policy?.externalActions || {};
  56:   return {
  57:     protectedBranches: Array.isArray(externalActions.protectedBranches) && externalActions.protectedBranches.length > 0
  58:       ? externalActions.protectedBranches
  59:       : DEFAULT_PROTECTED_BRANCHES,
  60:     hard: externalActions.hard || DEFAULT_HARD,
  61:     soft: externalActions.soft || DEFAULT_SOFT,
  62:   };
  63: }
  64: 
  65: function getTier(label, policy) {
  66:   if (policy.hard.includes(label)) return 'hard';
  67:   if (policy.soft.includes(label)) return 'soft';
  68:   return 'allow';
  69: }
  70: 
  71: function escapeRegExp(str) {
  72:   return str.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
  73: }
  74: 
  75: function checkProtectedBranchDeletion(command, protectedBranches) {
  76:   if (protectedBranches.length === 0) return null;
  77: 
  78:   for (const branch of protectedBranches) {
  79:     const escaped = escapeRegExp(branch);
  80:     const pushDeleteRe = new RegExp(`\\bgit\\s+push\\s+.*--delete\\s+${escaped}\\b`);
  81:     const pushColonRe = new RegExp(`\\bgit\\s+push\\s+\\S+\\s+:${escaped}\\b`);
  82:     const branchDeleteRe = new RegExp(`\\bgit\\s+branch\\s+-[dD]\\s+${escaped}\\b`);
  83: 
  84:     if (pushDeleteRe.test(command) || pushColonRe.test(command) || branchDeleteRe.test(command)) {
  85:       return branch;
  86:     }
  87:   }
  88: 
  89:   return null;
  90: }
  91: 
  92: function stripQuotedContent(command) {
  93:   let stripped = command;
  94:   stripped = stripped.replace(/<<-?\s*'?(\w+)'?[^\n]*\n[\s\S]*?\n\s*\1\b/g, '');
  95:   stripped = stripped.replace(/"\$\([\s\S]*?\)"/g, '""');
  96:   stripped = stripped.replace(/'\$\([\s\S]*?\)'/g, "''");
  97:   stripped = stripped.replace(/`[^`]*`/g, '``');
  98:   stripped = stripped.replace(/"(?:[^"\\]|\\.)*"/g, '""');
  99:   stripped = stripped.replace(/'(?:[^'\\]|\\.)*'/g, "''");
 100:   return stripped;
 101: }
 102: 
 103: function detectExternalAction(command, policy) {
 104:   const stripped = stripQuotedContent(command);
 105: 
 106:   for (const { regex, label } of SECRETS_PATTERNS) {
 107:     if (regex.test(stripped)) {
 108:       return { kind: 'secret', label, tier: 'secrets', stripped };
 109:     }
 110:   }
 111: 
 112:   const deletedBranch = checkProtectedBranchDeletion(stripped, policy.protectedBranches);
 113:   if (deletedBranch) {
 114:     return {
 115:       kind: 'protected-branch',
 116:       label: `delete ${deletedBranch}`,
 117:       branch: deletedBranch,
 118:       tier: 'protected-branch',
 119:       stripped,
 120:     };
 121:   }
 122: 
 123:   for (const { regex, label } of ALL_PATTERNS) {
 124:     if (!regex.test(stripped)) continue;
 125:     return { kind: 'external-action', label, tier: getTier(label, policy), stripped };
 126:   }
 127: 
 128:   return null;
 129: }
 130:

 132:   ALL_PATTERNS,
 133:   DEFAULT_HARD,
 134:   DEFAULT_PROTECTED_BRANCHES,
 135:   DEFAULT_SOFT,

 137:   checkProtectedBranchDeletion,
 138:   detectExternalAction,

 141:   readExternalActionPolicy,
 142:   stripQuotedContent,

  27:   const options = {
  28:     projectRoot: process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd(),
  29:     hookProfile: process.env.CITADEL_CLAUDE_HOOK_PROFILE || 'auto',

  34:     const arg = args[index];

   1: #!/usr/bin/env node

   3: /**
   4:  * codex-compat.js -- Generates all Codex CLI artifacts from Citadel source.
   5:  *
   6:  * Reads Citadel's skills, agents, hooks, MCP config, and package.json,
   7:  * then generates Codex-native equivalents:
   8:  *

  10:  *   .codex-plugin/plugin.json              Plugin manifest for Codex marketplace
  11:  *   .codex/agents/{name}.toml              Translated agent definitions

  17:  *   node scripts/codex-compat.js /path/to/project   # explicit project path
  18:  *   node scripts/codex-compat.js --dry-run           # show what would be generated
  19:  *
  20:  * Generated files include headers marking them as Citadel-managed.
  21:  * Re-running is idempotent -- overwrites generated files, never touches hand-authored ones.
  22:  */
  23: 
  24: 'use strict';
  25: 
  26: const fs   = require('fs');
  27: const path = require('path');
  28: 
  29: const CITADEL_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
  30: const args = process.argv.slice(2);
  31: const DRY_RUN = args.includes('--dry-run');
  32: const PROJECT_ROOT = args.find(a => !a.startsWith('--')) || process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd();
  33: 
  34: // ---- Utility helpers --------------------------------------------------------
  35: 
  36: function ensureDir(dir) {
  37:   if (!DRY_RUN && !fs.existsSync(dir)) {
  38:     fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  39:   }
  40: }
  41: 
  42: function writeFile(filePath, content) {
  43:   if (DRY_RUN) {
  44:     console.log(`  [dry-run] Would write: ${filePath}`);
  45:     return;
  46:   }
  47:   ensureDir(path.dirname(filePath));
  48:   fs.writeFileSync(filePath, content, 'utf8');
  49: }
  50: 
  51: function readJSON(filePath) {
  52:   try {
  53:     return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8'));
  54:   } catch {
  55:     return null;
  56:   }
  57: }
  58: 
  59: function parseFrontmatter(content) {
  60:   // Normalize line endings to \n for consistent parsing
  61:   const normalized = content.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
  62:   const match = normalized.match(/^---\n([\s\S]*?)\n---/);
  63:   if (!match) return {};
  64:   const fm = {};
  65:   for (const line of match[1].split('\n')) {
  66:     // Skip YAML list items (lines starting with -)
  67:     if (line.trim().startsWith('-')) continue;
  68:     const colonIdx = line.indexOf(':');
  69:     if (colonIdx === -1) continue;
  70:     const key = line.slice(0, colonIdx).trim();
  71:     // Skip indented keys (nested YAML)
  72:     if (line.match(/^\s+\w/)) continue;
  73:     let val = line.slice(colonIdx + 1).trim();
  74:     // Strip YAML quotes
  75:     if ((val.startsWith('"') && val.endsWith('"')) || (val.startsWith("'") && val.endsWith("'"))) {
  76:       val = val.slice(1, -1);
  77:     }
  78:     // Handle multiline description (>-)
  79:     if (val === '>-' || val === '>') {
  80:       continue;
  81:     }
  82:     if (key && val) fm[key] = val;
  83:   }
  84:   // Handle multiline description by regex
  85:   const descMatch = normalized.match(/description:\s*>-?\n([\s\S]*?)(?=\n[a-zA-Z][\w-]*:|\n---)/);
  86:   if (descMatch) {
  87:     fm.description = descMatch[1].replace(/\n\s*/g, ' ').trim();
  88:   }
  89:   return fm;
  90: }
  91: 
  92: // ---- 1. Generate .codex/config.toml -----------------------------------------
  93: 
  94: function generateConfigToml() {
  95:   console.log('Generating .codex/config.toml...');
  96: 
  97:   // Read .mcp.json for MCP server entries
  98:   let mcpSection = '';
  99:   const mcpPath = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.mcp.json');
 100:   const mcpData = readJSON(mcpPath);
 101:   if (mcpData && mcpData.mcpServers) {
 102:     const entries = [];
 103:     for (const [name, config] of Object.entries(mcpData.mcpServers)) {
 104:       // Skip comments and disabled entries (prefixed with _)
 105:       if (name.startsWith('_')) continue;
 106:       entries.push(mcpServerToToml(name, config));
 107:     }
 108:     if (entries.length > 0) {
 109:       mcpSection = '\n' + entries.join('\n');
 110:     }
 111:   }
 112: 
 113:   // On Windows, PowerShell 5 fails to load its managed runtime in some environments
 114:   // (error 8009001d). Emit the [windows] agent_shell override and set SHELL in the
 115:   // env policy so both the Codex shell selector and any sub-invocations use Git Bash.
 116:   let shellEnvVars = 'CITADEL_RUNTIME = "codex"';
 117:   let windowsSection = '';
 118:   if (process.platform === 'win32') {
 119:     const gitBash = 'C:/Program Files/Git/bin/bash.exe';

 123: # Route Codex shell execution through Git Bash instead of PowerShell on Windows.
 124: # Prevents "Loading managed Windows PowerShell failed with error 8009001d" errors.
 125: [windows]
 126: agent_shell = "git-bash"

 128:     }

 133: 
 134: # Required feature flags for Citadel harness

 153: set = { ${shellEnvVars} }
 154: exclude = ["*SECRET*", "*TOKEN*", "*KEY*"]
 155: ${mcpSection}${windowsSection}`;
 156: 
 157:   writeFile(path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.codex', 'config.toml'), toml);
 158: }
 159: 
 160: // ---- MCP server translation -------------------------------------------------
 161: //
 162: // Claude Code's `.mcp.json` and Codex's `[mcp_servers.NAME]` TOML overlap on
 163: // most fields but diverge on a few. The translator below:
 164: //
 165: //   1. Maps shared fields directly (command, args, url, env, cwd).
 166: //   2. Maps Claude `headers` → Codex `http_headers`.
 167: //   3. Detects token-style env vars and emits `bearer_token_env_var` while
 168: //      ALSO keeping the env var in env (so the program can still read it
 169: //      directly if needed).
 170: //   4. Honors a `_codex` extension block on the server entry for Codex-only
 171: //      knobs (startup_timeout_sec, tool_timeout_sec, enabled_tools,
 172: //      disabled_tools, enabled, required, env_http_headers, etc.). Any
 173: //      key under `_codex` is emitted verbatim into the TOML, which means
 174: //      new Codex fields work the moment they're added to the config —
 175: //      no edit to this generator required.
 176: //   5. Surfaces unrecognized top-level keys as a warning so silent drops
 177: //      become visible.
 178: //
 179: // Future-proofing: when Claude or Codex adds new common keys, prefer
 180: // extending CLAUDE_TO_CODEX_MAP. When Codex adds new Codex-only knobs,
 181: // users can pass them through `_codex` immediately; we tighten the
 182: // generator on the next pass.
 183: 
 184: const CLAUDE_TO_CODEX_MAP = {
 185:   command: 'command',
 186:   args: 'args',
 187:   url: 'url',
 188:   cwd: 'cwd',
 189:   env: 'env',
 190:   headers: 'http_headers',
 191: };
 192: 
 193: function tomlEscape(str) {
 194:   return String(str).replace(/\\/g, '\\\\').replace(/"/g, '\\"');
 195: }
 196: 
 197: function tomlValue(value) {
 198:   if (value === null || value === undefined) return null;
 199:   if (typeof value === 'string') return `"${tomlEscape(value)}"`;
 200:   if (typeof value === 'number' || typeof value === 'boolean') return String(value);
 201:   if (Array.isArray(value)) {
 202:     if (value.length === 0) return '[]';
 203:     return '[' + value.map(tomlValue).filter((v) => v !== null).join(', ') + ']';
 204:   }
 205:   if (typeof value === 'object') {
 206:     const inner = Object.entries(value)
 207:       .filter(([k]) => !k.startsWith('_'))
 208:       .map(([k, v]) => `${k} = ${tomlValue(v)}`)
 209:       .filter((line) => !line.endsWith(' = null'));
 210:     return '{ ' + inner.join(', ') + ' }';
 211:   }
 212:   return null;
 213: }
 214: 
 215: function mcpServerToToml(name, config) {
 216:   const lines = [`[mcp_servers.${name}]`];
 217:   const warnings = [];
 218: 
 219:   // Map common fields (Claude shape → Codex shape)
 220:   for (const [claudeKey, codexKey] of Object.entries(CLAUDE_TO_CODEX_MAP)) {
 221:     if (!(claudeKey in config) || config[claudeKey] === null || config[claudeKey] === undefined) continue;
 222:     const value = config[claudeKey];
 223:     // Skip empty arrays/objects to keep the TOML tidy.
 224:     if (Array.isArray(value) && value.length === 0) continue;
 225:     if (typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value) && Object.keys(value).length === 0) continue;
 226:     const rendered = tomlValue(value);
 227:     if (rendered !== null) lines.push(`${codexKey} = ${rendered}`);
 228:   }
 229: 
 230:   // Token-style env detection: emit bearer_token_env_var as a hint to Codex
 231:   // for HTTP servers, but keep the env var in `env` (handled above) so stdio
 232:   // servers still see it.
 233:   if (config.env && typeof config.env === 'object') {
 234:     for (const k of Object.keys(config.env)) {
 235:       if (k.startsWith('_')) continue;
 236:       if (/token/i.test(k) && config.url) {
 237:         lines.push(`bearer_token_env_var = "${tomlEscape(k)}"`);
 238:         break; // only one bearer slot per server
 239:       }
 240:     }
 241:   }
 242: 
 243:   // Codex-only extensions: anything under `_codex` is emitted verbatim.
 244:   // This is the forward-compatibility hatch — when OpenAI ships a new
 245:   // [mcp_servers.*] field, users can set it without touching this script.
 246:   if (config._codex && typeof config._codex === 'object') {
 247:     for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(config._codex)) {
 248:       if (v === null || v === undefined) continue;
 249:       const rendered = tomlValue(v);
 250:       if (rendered !== null) lines.push(`${k} = ${rendered}`);
 251:     }
 252:   }
 253: 
 254:   // Warn about unrecognized top-level keys so we notice when Claude's
 255:   // shape evolves.
 256:   const known = new Set([...Object.keys(CLAUDE_TO_CODEX_MAP), '_codex', 'type']);
 257:   for (const k of Object.keys(config)) {
 258:     if (k.startsWith('_')) continue;
 259:     if (!known.has(k)) warnings.push(`mcp_servers.${name}: unrecognized field "${k}" — not translated`);
 260:   }
 261:   for (const w of warnings) console.warn(`  warning: ${w}`);
 262: 
 263:   lines.push('');
 264:   return lines.join('\n');
 265: }
 266: 
 267: // ---- 2. Generate .codex-plugin/plugin.json ----------------------------------

 269: function generatePluginManifest() {
 270:   console.log('Generating .codex-plugin/plugin.json...');
 271: 
 272:   const pkg = readJSON(path.join(CITADEL_ROOT, 'package.json')) || {};
 273:   const repoUrl = (pkg.repository && pkg.repository.url) || '';
 274: 
 275:   // Count skills
 276:   const skillsDir = path.join(CITADEL_ROOT, 'skills');
 277:   let skillCount = 0;
 278:   if (fs.existsSync(skillsDir)) {
 279:     skillCount = fs.readdirSync(skillsDir).filter(d =>
 280:       fs.existsSync(path.join(skillsDir, d, 'SKILL.md'))
 281:     ).length;
 282:   }
 283: 
 284:   const manifest = {
 285:     name: pkg.name || 'citadel',
 286:     version: pkg.version || '1.0.0',
 287:     description: pkg.description || 'Agent orchestration harness',
 288:     author: {
 289:       name: pkg.author || 'Citadel',
 290:       url: repoUrl.replace(/\.git$/, ''),
 291:     },
 292:     license: pkg.license || 'MIT',
 293:     keywords: pkg.keywords || ['orchestration', 'campaigns', 'fleet'],
 294:     skills: './skills/',
 295:     interface: {
 296:       displayName: 'Citadel Harness',
 297:       shortDescription: `Agent orchestration: ${skillCount} skills, campaigns, fleet coordination, quality gates`,
 298:       category: 'Developer Tools',
 299:       capabilities: [
 300:         'orchestration',
 301:         'campaigns',
 302:         'parallel-agents',
 303:         'quality-gates',
 304:         'telemetry',
 305:         'intent-routing',
 306:       ],
 307:     },
 308:   };
 309: 
 310:   // Add mcpServers pointer if .mcp.json exists
 311:   if (fs.existsSync(path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.mcp.json'))) {
 312:     manifest.mcpServers = './.mcp.json';
 313:   }
 314: 
 315:   const content = '// Generated by Citadel. Do not edit manually.\n'
 316:     + JSON.stringify(manifest, null, 2) + '\n';
 317: 
 318:   writeFile(path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.codex-plugin', 'plugin.json'), content);
 319: }
 320: 
 321: // ---- 3. Translate agent definitions -----------------------------------------
 322: //
 323: // Agent bodies become `developer_instructions` strings inside TOML. Codex has
 324: // historically had a soft cap on how much instruction text it will load; we
 325: // truncate to keep TOML files reasonable. Two failure modes the previous
 326: // implementation hit:
 327: //
 328: //   1. The truncation happened silently — agents like `archon` (>4000 chars)
 329: //      lost load-bearing guidance with no console output.
 330: //   2. The 4000-char number was hardcoded, so when Codex raises its limit
 331: //      (or if it never had one for a particular agent) we couldn't relax it
 332: //      without editing this file.
 333: //
 334: // Now: configurable, observable, per-agent override.
 335: 
 336: const DEFAULT_AGENT_MAX_CHARS = 4000;
 337: 
 338: function getAgentMaxChars(fm) {
 339:   // Per-agent override via frontmatter takes precedence — useful for an agent
 340:   // whose definition really needs more room.
 341:   if (fm.codex_max_chars !== undefined) {
 342:     const n = Number(fm.codex_max_chars);
 343:     if (Number.isFinite(n) && n >= 0) return n;
 344:   }
 345:   // Project-wide override via env var.
 346:   const envVal = process.env.CITADEL_CODEX_AGENT_MAX_CHARS;
 347:   if (envVal !== undefined && envVal !== '') {
 348:     const n = Number(envVal);
 349:     if (Number.isFinite(n) && n >= 0) return n;
 350:   }
 351:   return DEFAULT_AGENT_MAX_CHARS;
 352: }
 353: 
 354: function translateAgents() {
 355:   console.log('Translating agent definitions...');
 356: 
 357:   const agentsDir = path.join(CITADEL_ROOT, 'agents');
 358:   if (!fs.existsSync(agentsDir)) {
 359:     console.log('  No agents/ directory found, skipping.');
 360:     return;
 361:   }
 362: 
 363:   const agentFiles = fs.readdirSync(agentsDir).filter(f => f.endsWith('.md'));
 364: 
 365:   for (const file of agentFiles) {
 366:     const content = fs.readFileSync(path.join(agentsDir, file), 'utf8');
 367:     const fm = parseFrontmatter(content);
 368:     if (!fm.name) continue;
 369: 
 370:     const toml = agentToToml(fm, content);
 371:     writeFile(
 372:       path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.codex', 'agents', `${fm.name}.toml`),
 373:       toml
 374:     );
 375:     console.log(`  ${file} -> .codex/agents/${fm.name}.toml`);
 376:   }
 377: }
 378: 
 379: function agentToToml(fm, fullContent) {
 380:   // Extract the body after frontmatter as developer_instructions
 381:   const normalized = fullContent.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
 382:   const bodyMatch = normalized.match(/^---\n[\s\S]*?\n---\n([\s\S]*)/);
 383:   const body = bodyMatch ? bodyMatch[1].trim() : '';
 384: 
 385:   // Truncate instructions, but loudly. Set CITADEL_CODEX_AGENT_MAX_CHARS=0
 386:   // (or higher than the body length) to disable.
 387:   const maxChars = getAgentMaxChars(fm);
 388:   let instructions = body;
 389:   if (maxChars > 0 && body.length > maxChars) {
 390:     const droppedChars = body.length - maxChars;
 391:     console.warn(
 392:       `  warning: agent "${fm.name}" instructions truncated — dropped ${droppedChars} chars ` +
 393:       `(body=${body.length}, cap=${maxChars}). Set codex_max_chars in frontmatter or ` +
 394:       `CITADEL_CODEX_AGENT_MAX_CHARS env to raise the cap.`
 395:     );
 396:     instructions = body.slice(0, maxChars) +
 397:       `\n\n[Truncated by Citadel adapter — ${droppedChars} chars dropped. See agents/${fm.name}.md for the full definition.]`;
 398:   }
 399: 
 400:   // Map Citadel model names to Codex model names
 401:   const modelMap = {
 402:     'opus':   'gpt-5.4',
 403:     'sonnet': 'gpt-5.4-mini',
 404:     'haiku':  'gpt-5.4-mini',
 405:   };
 406:   const model = modelMap[fm.model] || 'gpt-5.4';
 407: 
 408:   // Map effort to reasoning_effort
 409:   const effortMap = {
 410:     'high':   'high',
 411:     'medium': 'medium',
 412:     'low':    'low',
 413:   };
 414:   const effort = effortMap[fm.effort] || 'high';
 415: 
 416:   const lines = [
 417:     '# Generated by Citadel. Do not edit manually.',
 418:     `# Source: agents/${fm.name}.md`,
 419:     '',
 420:     `name = "${fm.name}"`,
 421:     `description = "${(fm.description || '').replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`,
 422:     `model = "${model}"`,
 423:     `model_reasoning_effort = "${effort}"`,
 424:     '',
 425:     `developer_instructions = """`,
 426:     instructions,
 427:     `"""`,
 428:   ];
 429: 
 430:   return lines.join('\n') + '\n';
 431: }
 432: 
 433: // ---- 4. Sync skills to Codex discovery path ---------------------------------
 434: 
 435: // Files and directories that should never travel into .agents/skills/.
 436: // Everything else in a skill directory is copied — so when a skill grows
 437: // scripts/, references/, assets/, or __benchmarks__/, they show up in Codex
 438: // without further changes here.
 439: const SKILL_COPY_EXCLUDES = new Set([
 440:   'node_modules',
 441:   '.git',
 442:   '.DS_Store',
 443:   'Thumbs.db',
 444:   // 'agents' is excluded because we generate agents/openai.yaml ourselves;
 445:   // any hand-authored sibling under agents/ would be clobbered or would
 446:   // race the generator. If a skill needs a checked-in agents/ subtree,
 447:   // revisit this exclusion.
 448:   'agents',
 449: ]);
 450: 
 451: function copySkillTree(sourceDir, targetDir) {
 452:   if (DRY_RUN) {
 453:     console.log(`  [dry-run] Would copy skill tree: ${sourceDir} -> ${targetDir}`);
 454:     return;
 455:   }
 456:   // fs.cpSync requires Node 16.7+. The project documents Node 18+ as the
 457:   // minimum (see Codex install guide), so this is safe.
 458:   fs.cpSync(sourceDir, targetDir, {
 459:     recursive: true,
 460:     force: true,
 461:     errorOnExist: false,
 462:     filter: (src) => !SKILL_COPY_EXCLUDES.has(path.basename(src)),
 463:   });
 464: }
 465: 
 466: function syncSkills() {
 467:   console.log('Syncing skills to .agents/skills/...');
 468: 
 469:   const sourceDir = path.join(CITADEL_ROOT, 'skills');
 470:   if (!fs.existsSync(sourceDir)) {
 471:     console.log('  No skills/ directory found, skipping.');
 472:     return;
 473:   }
 474: 
 475:   const targetBase = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.agents', 'skills');
 476:   const skillDirs = fs.readdirSync(sourceDir).filter(d =>
 477:     fs.existsSync(path.join(sourceDir, d, 'SKILL.md'))
 478:   );
 479: 
 480:   let synced = 0;
 481:   for (const skillName of skillDirs) {
 482:     const sourceSkillDir = path.join(sourceDir, skillName);
 483:     const sourcePath = path.join(sourceSkillDir, 'SKILL.md');
 484:     const targetDir  = path.join(targetBase, skillName);
 485: 
 486:     // Copy the entire skill directory (SKILL.md + scripts/, references/,
 487:     // assets/, __benchmarks__/, etc.). Excluded names from SKILL_COPY_EXCLUDES
 488:     // are skipped at every depth via the filter callback.
 489:     copySkillTree(sourceSkillDir, targetDir);
 490: 
 491:     // Generate openai.yaml from frontmatter — written after the copy so
 492:     // it overlays anything that might have slipped in.
 493:     const content = fs.readFileSync(sourcePath, 'utf8');
 494:     const fm = parseFrontmatter(content);
 495:     if (fm.name || fm.description) {
 496:       generateOpenAIYaml(targetDir, fm);
 497:     }
 498: 
 499:     synced++;
 500:   }
 501: 
 502:   console.log(`  ${synced} skills synced (full directory tree).`);
 503: }
 504: 
 505: function generateOpenAIYaml(skillDir, fm) {
 506:   const yaml = `# Generated by Citadel. Do not edit manually.
 507: interface:
 508:   display_name: "${(fm.name || 'unknown').replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"
 509:   short_description: "${(fm.description || '').replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"
 510: policy:
 511:   allow_implicit_invocation: true
 512: `;
 513: 
 514:   writeFile(path.join(skillDir, 'agents', 'openai.yaml'), yaml);
 515: }
 516:

 522:   const agentsMdPath = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, 'AGENTS.md');
 523:   if (fs.existsSync(agentsMdPath)) {
 524:     console.log('  AGENTS.md already exists, skipping.');
 525:     return;

 532:     writeFile(agentsMdPath, content);

 536:   }

   7:   'PostToolUse',
   8:   'PostToolUseFailure',

  16: function parseClaudeVersion(raw) {
  17:   if (typeof raw !== 'string') return null;
  18:   const match = raw.match(/(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)/);
  19:   return match ? match[1] : null;
  20: }
  21: 
  22: function compareVersions(left, right) {
  23:   const a = String(left).split('.').map((part) => Number(part) || 0);
  24:   const b = String(right).split('.').map((part) => Number(part) || 0);
  25:   const length = Math.max(a.length, b.length);
  26: 
  27:   for (let index = 0; index < length; index++) {
  28:     const diff = (a[index] || 0) - (b[index] || 0);
  29:     if (diff !== 0) return diff > 0 ? 1 : -1;
  30:   }
  31: 
  32:   return 0;
  33: }
  34: 
  35: function detectClaudeVersion(claudeBin = 'claude') {
  36:   try {
  37:     const output = execFileSync(claudeBin, ['--version'], {
  38:       stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
  39:       encoding: 'utf8',
  40:       timeout: 5000,
  41:     });
  42:     return {
  43:       version: parseClaudeVersion(output),
  44:       source: 'cli',
  45:       raw: String(output || '').trim(),
  46:     };
  47:   } catch {
  48:     return {
  49:       version: null,
  50:       source: 'unavailable',
  51:       raw: '',
  52:     };
  53:   }
  54: }

  56: function getSupportedEventsForVersion(version, templateEvents) {
  57:   const supported = new Set(SAFE_EVENTS.filter((event) => templateEvents.includes(event)));
  58: 
  59:   if (!version) {
  60:     return supported;
  61:   }
  62: 
  63:   if (compareVersions(version, '2.1.76') >= 0) {
  64:     supported.add('PostCompact');
  65:   }
  66: 
  67:   if (compareVersions(version, '2.1.78') >= 0) {
  68:     supported.add('StopFailure');
  69:   }
  70: 
  71:   if (compareVersions(version, '2.1.83') >= 0) {
  72:     supported.add('TaskCompleted');
  73:   }
  74:

  78:     supported.add('WorktreeRemove');
  79:   }

  84: function selectSupportedClaudeHookEvents(options = {}) {

  94:       skippedEvents: [],

  96:     };

 108:     };
 109:   }

   1: 'use strict';
   2: 
   3: function parseFrontmatter(content) {
   4:   const match = content.match(/^---\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n---/);
   5:   if (!match) return {};
   6: 
   7:   const result = {};
   8:   for (const line of match[1].split(/\r?\n/)) {
   9:     const kv = line.match(/^(\w+):\s*(.*)$/);
  10:     if (!kv) continue;
  11:     const raw = kv[2].trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, '');
  12:     result[kv[1]] = raw !== '' && !Number.isNaN(Number(raw)) ? Number(raw) : raw;
  13:   }
  14: 
  15:   return result;
  16: }
  17: 
  18: function parseSection(content, sectionName) {
  19:   const escaped = sectionName.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
  20:   const regex = new RegExp(`^##\\s+${escaped}\\s*\\r?\\n([\\s\\S]*?)(?=^##\\s+|(?![\\s\\S]))`, 'm');
  21:   const match = content.match(regex);
  22:   return match ? match[1] : null;
  23: }
  24: 
  25: function parseBulletSection(content, sectionName) {
  26:   const section = parseSection(content, sectionName);
  27:   if (!section) return [];
  28:   return section
  29:     .split(/\r?\n/)
  30:     .map(line => line.replace(/^[-*\s]+/, '').trim())
  31:     .filter(Boolean);
  32: }
  33: 
  34: /**
  35:  * Parse the Phases markdown table into structured objects.
  36:  *
  37:  * Expects a table with columns (in any order):
  38:  *   | # | Status | Type | Phase | Done When |
  39:  *
  40:  * Returns an array of phase objects. Rows that don't parse cleanly are skipped.
  41:  *
  42:  * @param {string} content - Full campaign file content
  43:  * @returns {Array<{number: number, status: string, type: string, name: string, doneWhen: string}>}
  44:  */
  45: function parsePhaseTable(content) {
  46:   const section = parseSection(content, 'Phases');
  47:   if (!section) return [];
  48: 
  49:   const rows = section.split(/\r?\n/).filter(line => line.trim().startsWith('|'));
  50:   if (rows.length < 2) return [];
  51: 
  52:   // First row is the header — extract column positions
  53:   const headerCells = rows[0].split('|').map(c => c.trim().toLowerCase());
  54:   const col = {
  55:     number: headerCells.indexOf('#'),
  56:     status: headerCells.indexOf('status'),
  57:     type: headerCells.indexOf('type'),
  58:     name: headerCells.findIndex(h => h === 'phase' || h === 'name'),
  59:     doneWhen: headerCells.findIndex(h => h.includes('done') || h.includes('when')),
  60:   };
  61: 
  62:   // Second row is the separator — skip it
  63:   const dataRows = rows.slice(2);
  64:   const phases = [];
  65: 
  66:   for (const row of dataRows) {
  67:     const cells = row.split('|').map(c => c.trim());
  68:     // cells[0] is empty (before first |), col indices from indexOf already
  69:     // account for this empty slot so use them directly — no +1 adjustment.
  70:     const get = (idx) => (idx >= 0 && idx < cells.length ? cells[idx] : '');
  71:     const num = parseInt(get(col.number), 10);
  72:     if (Number.isNaN(num)) continue;
  73:     phases.push({
  74:       number: num,
  75:       status: get(col.status) || 'pending',
  76:       type: get(col.type) || '',
  77:       name: get(col.name) || '',
  78:       doneWhen: get(col.doneWhen) || '',
  79:     });
  80:   }
  81: 
  82:   return phases;
  83: }
  84: 
  85: function parseCampaignContent(content, options = {}) {
  86:   const slug = options.slug || null;
  87:   const frontmatter = parseFrontmatter(content);
  88:   const bodyStatusMatch = content.match(/^Status:\s*(\S+)$/im);
  89:   const titleMatch = content.match(/^#\s+Campaign:\s*(.+)$/im);
  90: 
  91:   return {
  92:     slug,
  93:     content,
  94:     frontmatter,
  95:     title: titleMatch ? titleMatch[1].trim() : slug,
  96:     bodyStatus: bodyStatusMatch ? bodyStatusMatch[1].trim() : null,
  97:     phases: parsePhaseTable(content),
  98:     claimedScope: parseBulletSection(content, 'Claimed Scope'),
  99:     restrictedFiles: parseBulletSection(content, 'Restricted Files'),
 100:   };
 101: }

 104:   parseCampaignContent,

 107:   parseSection,

   3: /**
   4:  * discovery-writer.js — Write structured agent discoveries to a persistent JSONL store.
   5:  *
   6:  * Each agent that completes (via Fleet or Daemon) writes one record here.
   7:  * Records accumulate across sessions. The momentum synthesizer reads them all
   8:  * to build a cross-session signal that Fleet injects into Wave 1 context.
   9:  *
  10:  * File layout: .planning/discoveries/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl (daily rotation)
  11:  */
  12: 
  13: const fs = require('fs');
  14: const path = require('path');
  15: 
  16: const SCHEMA_VERSION = 1;
  17: 
  18: function parseArgs(argv) {
  19:   const args = {};
  20:   for (let i = 2; i < argv.length; i++) {
  21:     const key = argv[i];
  22:     const val = argv[i + 1];
  23:     if (key === '--session') { args.session = val; i++; }
  24:     else if (key === '--agent') { args.agent = val; i++; }
  25:     else if (key === '--wave') { args.wave = parseInt(val, 10); i++; }
  26:     else if (key === '--status') { args.status = val; i++; }
  27:     else if (key === '--scope') { args.scope = val; i++; }
  28:     else if (key === '--handoff') {
  29:       try { args.handoff_items = JSON.parse(val); } catch { args.handoff_items = []; }
  30:       i++;
  31:     }
  32:     else if (key === '--decisions') {
  33:       try { args.decisions = JSON.parse(val); } catch { args.decisions = []; }
  34:       i++;
  35:     }
  36:     else if (key === '--files') {
  37:       try { args.files_touched = JSON.parse(val); } catch { args.files_touched = []; }
  38:       i++;
  39:     }
  40:     else if (key === '--failures') {
  41:       try { args.failures = JSON.parse(val); } catch { args.failures = []; }
  42:       i++;
  43:     }
  44:   }
  45:   return args;
  46: }
  47: 
  48: /**
  49:  * Write one discovery record.
  50:  * @param {string} projectRoot - Absolute path to project root
  51:  * @param {object} entry - Discovery fields
  52:  * @returns {{ file: string, record: object }}
  53:  */
  54: function writeDiscovery(projectRoot, entry) {
  55:   const date = new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10);
  56:   const dir = path.join(projectRoot, '.planning', 'discoveries');
  57:   if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  58: 
  59:   const file = path.join(dir, `${date}.jsonl`);
  60:   const record = {
  61:     schema: SCHEMA_VERSION,
  62:     timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
  63:     session: entry.session || null,
  64:     agent: entry.agent || 'unknown',
  65:     wave: typeof entry.wave === 'number' && !isNaN(entry.wave) ? entry.wave : null,
  66:     scope: Array.isArray(entry.scope) ? entry.scope
  67:       : typeof entry.scope === 'string' ? entry.scope.split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)
  68:       : [],
  69:     status: entry.status || 'success',
  70:     handoff_items: Array.isArray(entry.handoff_items) ? entry.handoff_items : [],
  71:     decisions: Array.isArray(entry.decisions) ? entry.decisions : [],
  72:     files_touched: Array.isArray(entry.files_touched) ? entry.files_touched : [],
  73:     failures: Array.isArray(entry.failures) ? entry.failures : [],
  74:   };
  75: 
  76:   fs.appendFileSync(file, JSON.stringify(record) + '\n');
  77:   return { file, record };
  78: }
  79: 
  80: /**
  81:  * Read all discovery records from all daily JSONL files, sorted oldest-first.
  82:  * @param {string} projectRoot
  83:  * @returns {object[]}
  84:  */
  85: function readAllDiscoveries(projectRoot) {
  86:   const dir = path.join(projectRoot, '.planning', 'discoveries');
  87:   if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) return [];
  88: 
  89:   const records = [];
  90:   const files = fs.readdirSync(dir).filter(f => f.endsWith('.jsonl')).sort();
  91:   for (const file of files) {
  92:     const lines = fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, file), 'utf8').split('\n').filter(Boolean);
  93:     for (const line of lines) {
  94:       try { records.push(JSON.parse(line)); } catch { /* skip malformed */ }
  95:     }
  96:   }
  97:   return records;
  98: }

   7: function updateCampaignStatus(filePath, status) {
   8:   let content = fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8');
   9: 
  10:   if (/^(status:\s*).+$/im.test(content)) {
  11:     content = content.replace(/^(status:\s*).+$/im, `$1${status}`);
  12:   }
  13: 
  14:   if (/^(Status:\s*).+$/m.test(content)) {
  15:     content = content.replace(/^(Status:\s*).+$/m, `$1${status}`);
  16:   }
  17: 
  18:   fs.writeFileSync(filePath, content);
  19:   return readCampaignFile(filePath);
  20: }
  21: 
  22: /**
  23:  * Update the status cell of a specific phase row in the Phases table.
  24:  *
  25:  * Finds the row whose first data cell matches `phaseNumber`, replaces the
  26:  * Status cell in place, and writes the file. The rest of the row is untouched.
  27:  *
  28:  * Valid status values (by convention): pending, in-progress, design-complete,
  29:  * complete, partial, failed, skipped.
  30:  *
  31:  * @param {string} filePath    - Absolute path to the campaign markdown file
  32:  * @param {number} phaseNumber - Phase number to update (matches the # column)
  33:  * @param {string} newStatus   - New status string to write into the Status cell
  34:  * @returns {object} Updated campaign object from readCampaignFile
  35:  */
  36: function updatePhaseStatus(filePath, phaseNumber, newStatus) {
  37:   let content = fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8');
  38: 
  39:   // Find the Phases section and locate the matching row.
  40:   // Row format: | N | status | type | name | done when |
  41:   // We match the row by its leading number cell (| N |) and rewrite the
  42:   // second cell (Status) without touching anything else.
  43:   const rowPattern = new RegExp(
  44:     `(^\\|\\s*${phaseNumber}\\s*\\|\\s*)([^|]+)(\\|.*)$`,
  45:     'm'
  46:   );
  47: 
  48:   if (!rowPattern.test(content)) {
  49:     throw new Error(
  50:       `updatePhaseStatus: phase ${phaseNumber} not found in ${path.basename(filePath)}`
  51:     );
  52:   }
  53: 
  54:   content = content.replace(rowPattern, (_, pre, _oldStatus, rest) => {
  55:     // Preserve original padding width if possible
  56:     const padded = ` ${newStatus} `;
  57:     return `${pre}${padded}${rest}`;
  58:   });
  59: 
  60:   fs.writeFileSync(filePath, content);
  61:   return readCampaignFile(filePath);
  62: }
  63: 
  64: function archiveCampaign(filePath, projectRoot) {
  65:   const paths = getCampaignPaths(projectRoot);
  66:   fs.mkdirSync(paths.completedDir, { recursive: true });
  67:   const destination = path.join(paths.completedDir, path.basename(filePath));
  68:   fs.renameSync(filePath, destination);
  69:   return readCampaignFile(destination);
  70: }

   1: #!/usr/bin/env node
   2: 
   3: /**
   4:  * cost-tracker.js -- PostToolUse hook (all tools)
   5:  *
   6:  * Real-time session cost monitoring. Reads Claude Code's session JSONL
   7:  * to compute actual token spend. Silent most of the time -- only outputs
   8:  * a one-line summary when cost crosses a threshold.
   9:  *
  10:  * Design principles:
  11:  *   - Never nag. Surfaces at meaningful thresholds, not every tool call.
  12:  *   - Time-gated: checks at most once per CHECK_INTERVAL_MS (3 min default).
  13:  *   - One line of output max. No walls of text.
  14:  *   - Tracks burn rate ($/min over recent window) so users can spot runaway sessions.
  15:  *   - Writes state to .planning/telemetry/cost-tracker-state.json for persistence.
  16:  *
  17:  * Thresholds (configurable via policy.costTracker in harness.json):
  18:  *   $5, $15, $30, $50, $75, $100, $150, $200, $300, $500
  19:  *
  20:  * Output format:
  21:  *   [cost] $27.43 this session (23 min, $1.19/min) -- next alert at $30
  22:  *
  23:  * Exit codes:
  24:  *   0 = always (never blocks)
  25:  */
  26: 
  27: 'use strict';
  28: 
  29: const fs = require('fs');
  30: const path = require('path');
  31: 
  32: const PLUGIN_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
  33: const health = require('./harness-health-util');
  34: 
  35: const PROJECT_ROOT = health.PROJECT_ROOT;
  36: const TELEMETRY_DIR = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.planning', 'telemetry');
  37: const STATE_FILE = path.join(TELEMETRY_DIR, 'cost-tracker-state.json');
  38: 
  39: // Time gate: don't check more than once per interval
  40: const CHECK_INTERVAL_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000; // 3 minutes
  41: 
  42: // Default thresholds in dollars
  43: const DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS = [5, 15, 30, 50, 75, 100, 150, 200, 300, 500];
  44: 
  45: // Phase length warning threshold (minutes). Research: failure rate 4x-increases beyond 35 min.
  46: const PHASE_WARN_MINUTES = 35;
  47: 
  48: const CITADEL_UI = process.env.CITADEL_UI === 'true';
  49: 
  50: // ── State management ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  51: 
  52: function readState() {
  53:   try {
  54:     if (!fs.existsSync(STATE_FILE)) return null;
  55:     return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(STATE_FILE, 'utf8'));
  56:   } catch { return null; }
  57: }
  58: 
  59: function writeState(state) {
  60:   try {
  61:     if (!fs.existsSync(TELEMETRY_DIR)) {
  62:       fs.mkdirSync(TELEMETRY_DIR, { recursive: true });
  63:     }
  64:     fs.writeFileSync(STATE_FILE, JSON.stringify(state, null, 2));
  65:   } catch { /* non-critical */ }
  66: }
  67: 
  68: // ── Policy ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  69: 
  70: function readPolicy() {
  71:   try {
  72:     const config = health.readConfig();
  73:     // Support new telemetry block, then cost section, then legacy policy.costTracker
  74:     const tel = config?.telemetry || {};
  75:     const ct = config?.cost || config?.policy?.costTracker || {};
  76:     const mode = ct.mode || 'api';
  77: 
  78:     // telemetry.enabled=false or telemetry.costAlerts=false both disable alerts.
  79:     const telemetryEnabled = tel.enabled !== false && tel.costAlerts !== false;
  80: 
  81:     return {
  82:       enabled: telemetryEnabled && mode !== 'off' && ct.enabled !== false,
  83:       mode, // 'api' | 'pro' | 'max' | 'off'
  84:       thresholds: Array.isArray(ct.thresholds) ? ct.thresholds : DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS,
  85:       checkIntervalMs: ct.checkIntervalMs || CHECK_INTERVAL_MS,
  86:       campaignBudgetAlerts: ct.campaignBudgetAlerts !== false,
  87:     };
  88:   } catch {
  89:     return {
  90:       enabled: true, mode: 'api', thresholds: DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS,
  91:       checkIntervalMs: CHECK_INTERVAL_MS, campaignBudgetAlerts: true,
  92:     };
  93:   }
  94: }
  95: 
  96: // ── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  97: 
  98: function formatTokens(n) {
  99:   if (n >= 1_000_000) return `${(n / 1_000_000).toFixed(1)}M`;
 100:   if (n >= 1_000) return `${(n / 1_000).toFixed(1)}K`;
 101:   return String(n);
 102: }
 103: 
 104: /**
 105:  * Check if the active campaign has a budget and whether we're near it.
 106:  * Reads budget from campaign frontmatter, cumulative cost from session-costs.jsonl.
 107:  * Returns a one-line alert string or null.
 108:  */
 109: function checkCampaignBudget(sessionCost) {
 110:   try {
 111:     const campaignsDir = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, '.planning', 'campaigns');
 112:     if (!fs.existsSync(campaignsDir)) return null;
 113: 
 114:     // Find active campaign with a budget
 115:     const files = fs.readdirSync(campaignsDir).filter(f => f.endsWith('.md'));
 116:     let slug = null;
 117:     let budget = null;
 118: 
 119:     for (const f of files) {
 120:       const content = fs.readFileSync(path.join(campaignsDir, f), 'utf8');
 121:       if (!/^status:\s*active/mi.test(content)) continue;
 122:       slug = f.replace(/\.md$/, '');
 123:       const budgetMatch = content.match(/^budget:\s*(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)/mi);
 124:       if (budgetMatch) budget = parseFloat(budgetMatch[1]);
 125:       break;
 126:     }
 127: 
 128:     if (!slug || !budget) return null;
 129: 
 130:     // Sum session costs for this campaign from session-costs.jsonl
 131:     const costFile = path.join(TELEMETRY_DIR, 'session-costs.jsonl');
 132:     if (!fs.existsSync(costFile)) return null;
 133: 
 134:     let campaignTotal = 0;
 135:     const lines = fs.readFileSync(costFile, 'utf8').split('\n').filter(Boolean);
 136:     for (const line of lines) {
 137:       try {
 138:         const entry = JSON.parse(line);
 139:         if (entry.campaign_slug !== slug) continue;
 140:         // Prefer real_cost > override_cost > estimated_cost
 141:         campaignTotal += entry.real_cost ?? entry.override_cost ?? entry.estimated_cost ?? 0;
 142:       } catch { continue; }
 143:     }
 144: 
 145:     // Add current session cost (not yet written to JSONL)
 146:     campaignTotal += sessionCost;
 147: 
 148:     const pct = Math.round((campaignTotal / budget) * 100);
 149: 
 150:     // Read state to check if we already alerted at this level
 151:     const state = readState();
 152:     const lastBudgetPct = state?.lastBudgetPct || 0;
 153: 
 154:     if (pct >= 100 && lastBudgetPct < 100) {
 155:       return `[cost] Campaign "${slug}" budget exceeded: $${campaignTotal.toFixed(0)}/$${budget} (${pct}%)`;
 156:     }
 157:     if (pct >= 80 && lastBudgetPct < 80) {
 158:       return `[cost] Campaign "${slug}": $${campaignTotal.toFixed(0)}/$${budget} budget (${pct}%)`;
 159:     }
 160: 
 161:     return null;
 162:   } catch { return null; }
 163: }
 164:
