 167: function main() {
 168:   let input = '';
 169:   process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');
 170:   process.stdin.on('data', (chunk) => { input += chunk; });
 171:   process.stdin.on('end', () => {
 172:     try {
 173:       run();
 174:     } catch {
 175:       process.exit(0); // fail open
 176:     }
 177:   });
 178: }
 179: 
 180: function run() {
 181:   const policy = readPolicy();
 182:   if (!policy.enabled) {
 183:     process.exit(0);
 184:   }
 185: 
 186:   const now = Date.now();
 187:   const state = readState();
 188: 
 189:   // Time gate: skip if we checked recently
 190:   if (state && state.lastCheckMs && (now - state.lastCheckMs) < policy.checkIntervalMs) {
 191:     process.exit(0);
 192:   }
 193: 
 194:   // Load the Claude runtime token adapter
 195:   let sessionTokens;
 196:   try {
 197:     sessionTokens = require(path.join(PLUGIN_ROOT, 'runtimes', 'claude-code', 'adapters', 'session-tokens'));
 198:   } catch {
 199:     process.exit(0); // module not available
 200:   }
 201: 
 202:   // Find current session
 203:   const sessionId = sessionTokens.getCurrentSessionId();
 204:   if (!sessionId) {
 205:     process.exit(0);
 206:   }
 207: 
 208:   // Read real token data
 209:   const result = sessionTokens.readSessionTokens(sessionId);
 210:   if (!result || result.combined.messages === 0) {
 211:     writeState({ lastCheckMs: now, sessionId, lastThresholdIndex: -1 });
 212:     process.exit(0);
 213:   }
 214: 
 215:   const cost = sessionTokens.computeCost(result.combined);
 216:   const tokens = result.combined;
 217: 
 218:   // Calculate duration and burn rate
 219:   let durationMin = 0;
 220:   let burnRate = 0;
 221:   if (tokens.first_timestamp && tokens.last_timestamp) {
 222:     durationMin = Math.max(1, Math.round(
 223:       (new Date(tokens.last_timestamp) - new Date(tokens.first_timestamp)) / 60000
 224:     ));
 225:     burnRate = cost / durationMin;
 226:   }
 227: 
 228:   // Find which threshold we've crossed
 229:   const thresholds = policy.thresholds.sort((a, b) => a - b);
 230:   let currentThresholdIndex = -1;
 231:   for (let i = thresholds.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
 232:     if (cost >= thresholds[i]) {
 233:       currentThresholdIndex = i;
 234:       break;
 235:     }
 236:   }
 237: 
 238:   // Check if we've crossed a NEW threshold since last check
 239:   const lastIndex = (state && state.sessionId === sessionId)
 240:     ? (state.lastThresholdIndex ?? -1)
 241:     : -1;
 242: 
 243:   // Check campaign budget (informational only, never blocks)
 244:   // Done before state write so checkCampaignBudget can read prior state
 245:   let budgetAlert = null;
 246:   if (policy.campaignBudgetAlerts) {
 247:     budgetAlert = checkCampaignBudget(cost);
 248:   }
 249: 
 250:   // Phase length warning: fire once when session first exceeds 35 min.
 251:   // Research (Morph, 2026): failure rate 4x-increases beyond this boundary.
 252:   const phaseLengthWarned = state?.sessionId === sessionId && state?.phaseLengthWarned === true;
 253:   const phaseLengthAlert = (durationMin >= PHASE_WARN_MINUTES && !phaseLengthWarned)
 254:     ? `[phase] ${durationMin} min -- consider /compact to reset context and reduce task failure risk`
 255:     : null;
 256: 
 257:   // Save state regardless (includes budget and phase tracking)
 258:   writeState({
 259:     lastCheckMs: now,
 260:     sessionId,
 261:     lastThresholdIndex: currentThresholdIndex,
 262:     lastBudgetPct: budgetAlert ? (budgetAlert.includes('exceeded') ? 100 : 80) : (state?.lastBudgetPct || 0),
 263:     phaseLengthWarned: phaseLengthAlert ? true : (state?.phaseLengthWarned || false),
 264:     cost,
 265:     durationMin,
 266:     burnRate: Math.round(burnRate * 100) / 100,
 267:     messages: tokens.messages,
 268:     subagents: result.subagents.length,
 269:   });
 270: 
 271:   // Determine what to output
 272:   const crossedNewThreshold = currentThresholdIndex > lastIndex;
 273: 
 274:   if (!crossedNewThreshold && !budgetAlert && !phaseLengthAlert) {
 275:     process.exit(0);
 276:   }
 277: 
 278:   // Format the notification based on mode
 279:   const messages = [];
 280: 
 281:   if (crossedNewThreshold) {
 282:     const nextThreshold = currentThresholdIndex + 1 < thresholds.length
 283:       ? thresholds[currentThresholdIndex + 1]
 284:       : null;
 285: 
 286:     if (policy.mode === 'pro' || policy.mode === 'max') {
 287:       // Token-focused output for subscribers (no dollar amounts)
 288:       const totalTokens = tokens.input_tokens + tokens.output_tokens +
 289:         tokens.cache_creation_input_tokens + tokens.cache_read_input_tokens;
 290:       const cacheHitRate = tokens.cache_read_input_tokens > 0
 291:         ? Math.round(tokens.cache_read_input_tokens / (tokens.cache_read_input_tokens + tokens.input_tokens + tokens.cache_creation_input_tokens) * 100)
 292:         : 0;
 293:       const tokPerMin = durationMin > 0 ? Math.round(totalTokens / durationMin) : 0;
 294: 
 295:       messages.push(
 296:         `[usage] ${formatTokens(totalTokens)} tokens (${cacheHitRate}% cache hits, ${durationMin} min, ${formatTokens(tokPerMin)}/min, ${tokens.messages} msgs)`
 297:       );
 298:     } else {
 299:       // Dollar-focused output for API users
 300:       const costStr = '$' + cost.toFixed(2);
 301:       const rateStr = '$' + burnRate.toFixed(2) + '/min';
 302:       const nextStr = nextThreshold ? ` | next alert at $${nextThreshold}` : '';
 303: 
 304:       messages.push(
 305:         `[cost] ${costStr} this session (${durationMin} min, ${rateStr}, ${tokens.messages} msgs, ${result.subagents.length} agents)${nextStr}`
 306:       );
 307:     }
 308: 
 309:     health.logTiming('cost-tracker', 0, {
 310:       event: 'threshold-crossed',
 311:       cost,
 312:       threshold: thresholds[currentThresholdIndex],
 313:       durationMin,
 314:       burnRate: Math.round(burnRate * 100) / 100,
 315:     });
 316:   }
 317: 
 318:   if (budgetAlert) {
 319:     messages.push(budgetAlert);
 320:   }
 321: 
 322:   if (phaseLengthAlert) {
 323:     messages.push(phaseLengthAlert);
 324:   }
 325: 
 326:   const output = messages.join('\n');
 327: 
 328:   if (CITADEL_UI) {
 329:     process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({
 330:       hook: 'cost-tracker',
 331:       action: crossedNewThreshold ? 'threshold' : (budgetAlert ? 'budget-alert' : 'phase-length'),
 332:       message: output,
 333:       timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
 334:       data: {
 335:         cost,
 336:         durationMin,
 337:         burnRate: Math.round(burnRate * 100) / 100,
 338:         threshold: crossedNewThreshold ? thresholds[currentThresholdIndex] : null,
 339:         messages: tokens.messages,
 340:         subagents: result.subagents.length,
 341:         budgetAlert: budgetAlert || null,
 342:         phaseLengthAlert: phaseLengthAlert || null,
 343:       },
 344:     }));
 345:   } else {
 346:     process.stdout.write(output);
 347:   }
 348: 
 349:   process.exit(0);
 350: }

  20: function printSession(label, tokens, cost) {

  31:   }
  32: }

   4:   Deep cost exploration and transparency. Shows real token usage, session costs,

  11: 
  12: # /cost -- Session & Campaign Cost Explorer
  13: 
  14: ## When to Use
  15: 
  16: - `/cost` -- current session cost and burn rate
  17: - `/cost today` -- today's total spend
  18: - `/cost week` -- this week's spend
  19: - `/cost campaign {slug}` -- total spend for a specific campaign
  20: - `/cost all` -- lifetime cost summary
  21: - When /do routes "how much", "what's the cost", "spending", "tokens", "burn rate"
  22: 
  23: ## Inputs
  24: 
  25: Optional arguments parsed from user message:
  26: - `today` -- filter to today's sessions
  27: - `week` -- filter to last 7 days
  28: - `campaign {slug}` -- filter to a specific campaign
  29: - `all` -- show all-time data
  30: - No argument -- show current session
  31: 
  32: ## Protocol
  33: 
  34: ### Step 1: READ REAL DATA
  35: 
  36: Run the session-tokens.js script to get real token data:
  37: 
  38: ```bash
  39: node scripts/session-tokens.js              # current/latest session
  40: node scripts/session-tokens.js --today      # today's sessions
  41: node scripts/session-tokens.js --all        # all sessions (use for week/all/campaign)
  42: ```
  43: 
  44: Also read:
  45: - `.planning/telemetry/cost-tracker-state.json` for live burn rate
  46: - `.planning/telemetry/session-costs.jsonl` for campaign attribution
  47: - `scripts/pricing.json` to show which pricing is being used

  50: data and clearly mark output as "(estimated)".
  51: 
  52: ### Step 2: RENDER BASED ON SCOPE
  53:

  57: === Session Cost Report ===

  76: 
  77: Cache efficiency: {pct}% of input tokens served from cache
  78:   (Higher = more cost-efficient. Cache reads cost 10x less than fresh input.)
  79: 
  80: Pricing source: scripts/pricing.json (version {version})
  81: ```
  82:

  84:

 123: 
 124: After the cost data, add one of these contextual lines based on the numbers:
 125: 
 126: - If burn rate > $2/min: "Burn rate is high. Consider whether subagent-heavy work

 132: - Otherwise: no extra context needed.
 133: 
 134: ### Step 4: FRINGE CASES
 135: 
 136: **If scripts/session-tokens.js does not exist:**
 137: Fall back to session-costs.jsonl data. Show estimated costs with "(est)" marker.
 138: 
 139: **If no session data exists:**

 142: at ~/.claude/projects/. These are created automatically by Claude Code.
 143: ```
 144:

 147:

 151: The token counts shown here represent your usage volume, not billing.
 152: For rate limit awareness, token throughput matters more than dollar cost.
 153: ```

 155: ## Fringe Cases
 156: 
 157: - **Telemetry directory missing**: `.planning/telemetry/` does not exist — output: "No telemetry data found. Run any skill first to generate session data, then re-run /cost."
 158: - **Malformed telemetry JSON**: a `session-*.json` file fails to parse — output: "Telemetry file is corrupted. Delete `.planning/telemetry/session-*.json` and re-run the skill that generated it." Skip the bad file and continue with the rest.
 159: - **MCP cost API returns no data**: Claude Code is not tracking this session — output: "Session cost unavailable from MCP. Check that Claude Code is running with cost tracking enabled. Showing telemetry file data only." Fall back to session-costs.jsonl.
 160: - **All session files are from a different project**: project paths in the files do not match the current working directory — warn: "Session files found belong to a different project. You may be in the wrong directory." List the project paths found in the session files.
 161:

 163:

 165: **Reversibility:** green — read-only; no files modified
 166: **Trust gates:**
 167: - Any: full cost report, session data, campaign attribution.
 168: 
 169: ## Quality Gates
 170: 
 171: - Always show real data when available, estimated when not
 172: - Always label data source: (real) vs (est)
 173: - Never claim specific dollar savings from Citadel -- show raw hook facts instead
 174: - Suggest ccusage for features we don't replicate (charts, billing windows)
 175: - Round costs to 2 decimal places, tokens to nearest K/M
 176: - Total output must fit on one screen for current-session view

  14: ## Submitting Pull Requests
  15: 
  16: 1. Fork the repo
  17: 2. Create a branch from `main` (e.g., `fix/issue-10-description` or `feat/new-skill`)
  18: 3. Make your changes
  19: 4. Run `node hooks_src/smoke-test.js` to verify hooks are healthy
  20: 5. Open a PR against `main`
  21: 
  22: **Branch protection is enabled.** All changes go through a PR. Direct pushes to main are blocked.
  23: 
  24: ### What to watch out for
  25: 
  26: **Cross-platform compatibility.** Citadel runs on Windows, macOS, and Linux. Before submitting:
  27: 
  28: - Hook definitions live in `hooks/hooks-template.json` and are installed per-project via `scripts/install-hooks.js`
  29: - The Claude installer is compatibility-aware by default; use `--hook-profile latest` in tests or fixtures when you need the full modern hook surface deterministically
  30: - Do NOT hardcode `/bin/bash`, `/bin/sh`, or other Unix-only paths
  31: - Do NOT assume forward-slash path separators in Node scripts (use `path.join()`)
  32: - Test on your platform and note which platform you tested on in the PR
  33: 
  34: **Plugin architecture.** Citadel is distributed as a Claude Code plugin. Users install it once and it works across all projects — no per-project file copying required.
  35: 
  36: - Hook scripts live in `hooks_src/` and are installed per-project via `scripts/install-hooks.js`
  37: - The `init-project` SessionStart hook auto-scaffolds per-project state (`.planning/`, `.citadel/scripts/`)
  38: - Per-project configuration lives in `.claude/harness.json` (generated by `/do setup`)
  39: 
  40: ## Adding a New Skill
  41: 
  42: Skills live in `skills/{name}/SKILL.md` (one directory per skill). Every skill needs:
  43: 
  44: ```yaml
  45: ---
  46: name: skill-name
  47: description: >-
  48:   One or two sentences explaining what the skill does.
  49: user-invocable: true
  50: auto-trigger: false
  51: ---
  52: ```
  53: 
  54: Follow the patterns in existing skills. Read 2-3 before writing your own.
  55: 
  56: Users can also create project-level custom skills in their project's `.claude/skills/` directory using `/create-skill`.
  57: 
  58: ## Adding a New Hook
  59: 
  60: Hooks live in `hooks_src/`. Before adding one:
  61: 
  62: 1. Read `harness-health-util.js` for shared utilities (telemetry, config, validation)
  63: 2. Use `execFileSync` (not `execSync`) to avoid shell injection
  64: 3. Use `require('./harness-health-util')` for the project root path
  65: 4. Use `process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd()` for the user's project root
  66: 5. Add your hook to `hooks/hooks-template.json` — the installer resolves `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` at install time
  67: 6. If the hook requires a newer Claude Code event, update the compatibility gating in `runtimes/claude-code/generators/hook-support.js`
  68: 7. Run `node hooks_src/smoke-test.js` to make sure the smoke test picks it up
  69: 
  70: ## Plugin Directory Structure
  71: 
  72: ```
  73: citadel/
  74:   .claude-plugin/       # Plugin manifest
  75:   skills/               # Built-in skill definitions ({name}.md per skill)
  76:   agents/               # Sub-agent definitions
  77:   hooks/
  78:     hooks-template.json # Hook definitions (resolved by install-hooks.js)
  79:   hooks_src/            # Hook script implementations
  80:   scripts/              # Utility scripts (synced to projects by init-project)
  81:   .planning/
  82:     _templates/         # Templates (copied to projects by init-project)
  83:   .claude/
  84:     agent-context/      # Rules injected into sub-agents
  85:   docs/                 # Reference documentation
  86: ```
  87: 
  88: ## Opt-in Hooks
  89: 
  90: Some hooks are not included in the default `hooks/hooks-template.json`. They are available in `hooks_src/` for users who want them:
  91: 
  92: - **`external-action-gate.js`** — Blocks git push, PR creation, issue comments, and other external actions until the user approves. Add to your project's `.claude/settings.local.json`:
  93:   ```json
  94:   {
  95:     "hooks": {
  96:       "PreToolUse": [{
  97:         "matcher": "Bash",
  98:         "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "node '${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks_src/external-action-gate.js'", "timeout": 5 }]
  99:       }]
 100:     }
 101:   }
 102:   ```
 103: 
 104: - **`issue-monitor.js`** — Checks for new GitHub issues on session start. Add to `.claude/settings.local.json`:
 105:   ```json
 106:   {
 107:     "hooks": {
 108:       "SessionStart": [{
 109:         "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "node '${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks_src/issue-monitor.js'", "timeout": 20 }]
 110:       }]
 111:     }
 112:   }
 113:   ```
 114: 
 115: > **Migrating from copy-based install?** These hooks previously lived at `.claude/hooks/`. The paths in your `settings.local.json` need to change from `node .claude/hooks/external-action-gate.js` to the `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` form shown above.
 116: 
 117: ## Code Style
 118: 
 119: - Node.js scripts use CommonJS (`require`), not ESM
 120: - Keep hooks fast (under 5s for PreToolUse, under 30s for PostToolUse)
 121: - Fail-closed for security hooks (exit 2 on error), fail-open for non-critical hooks (exit 0 on error)
 122: - No external dependencies. Hooks use only Node built-ins.

   1: #!/usr/bin/env node
   2: /**
   3:  * ASCII Diagram Verifier
   4:  *
   5:  * Checks that an ASCII diagram has proper alignment:
   6:  * - Box corners form closed rectangles (ASCII and Unicode)
   7:  * - Vertical pipes in the same column align
   8:  * - Arrows are unbroken (no gaps in dash runs)
   9:  * - No mixed tabs/spaces
  10:  *
  11:  * Usage: node verify.cjs <file>
  12:  *        echo "diagram" | node verify.cjs --stdin
  13:  */
  14: 
  15: const fs = require('fs');
  16: 
  17: // Character sets for both ASCII and Unicode box-drawing
  18: const CORNERS_ASCII = new Set(['+']);
  19: const CORNERS_UNICODE = new Set(['╔', '╗', '╚', '╝', '╭', '╮', '╰', '╯', '┏', '┓', '┗', '┛', '┼', '╬', '╋']);
  20: const H_BORDERS = new Set(['-', '=', '─', '═', '━']);
  21: const V_BORDERS = new Set(['|', '│', '║', '┃']);
  22: const ARROW_H = new Set(['-', '─', '━', '=', '═']);
  23: const ARROW_HEAD_R = new Set(['>', '▶', '▸', '►']);
  24: const ARROW_HEAD_L = new Set(['<', '◀', '◂', '◄']);
  25: const ARROW_HEAD_D = new Set(['v', 'V', '▼', '▾']);
  26: const ARROW_HEAD_U = new Set(['^', '▲', '▴']);
  27: 
  28: function isCorner(ch) { return CORNERS_ASCII.has(ch) || CORNERS_UNICODE.has(ch); }
  29: function isHBorder(ch) { return H_BORDERS.has(ch); }
  30: function isVBorder(ch) { return V_BORDERS.has(ch); }
  31: 
  32: function charAt(lines, x, y) {
  33:   if (y < 0 || y >= lines.length) return '';
  34:   if (x < 0 || x >= lines[y].length) return '';
  35:   return lines[y][x];
  36: }
  37: 
  38: function verify(text) {
  39:   const lines = text.split('\n');
  40:   const issues = [];
  41: 
  42:   // Check 1: No tabs
  43:   lines.forEach((line, i) => {
  44:     if (line.includes('\t')) {
  45:       issues.push({ line: i + 1, type: 'tab', msg: `Line ${i + 1} contains tab characters — use spaces only` });
  46:     }
  47:   });
  48: 
  49:   // Check 2: Find ALL corners (ASCII + Unicode) and verify connectivity
  50:   const corners = [];
  51:   lines.forEach((line, y) => {
  52:     for (let x = 0; x < line.length; x++) {
  53:       if (isCorner(line[x])) corners.push({ x, y, ch: line[x] });
  54:     }
  55:   });
  56: 
  57:   corners.forEach(({ x, y, ch }) => {
  58:     const right = charAt(lines, x + 1, y);
  59:     const left = charAt(lines, x - 1, y);
  60:     const below = charAt(lines, x, y + 1);
  61:     const above = charAt(lines, x, y - 1);
  62: 
  63:     const hasRight = isHBorder(right) || isCorner(right);
  64:     const hasLeft = isHBorder(left) || isCorner(left);
  65:     const hasBelow = isVBorder(below) || isCorner(below);
  66:     const hasAbove = isVBorder(above) || isCorner(above);
  67: 
  68:     const connections = [hasRight, hasLeft, hasBelow, hasAbove].filter(Boolean).length;
  69:     // 0 connections: likely a text character (e.g., "+ sealed"), not a corner — skip
  70:     // 1 connection: probably a misaligned corner — flag it
  71:     // For ASCII '+', also check if it's clearly text (surrounded by alphanumeric/space)
  72:     if (connections === 1) {
  73:       issues.push({
  74:         line: y + 1, col: x + 1, type: 'corner',
  75:         msg: `Corner '${ch}' at (${y + 1}:${x + 1}) only connects in 1 direction — may be misaligned`
  76:       });
  77:     } else if (connections === 0 && ch !== '+') {
  78:       // Unicode corners with 0 connections are definitely misaligned
  79:       issues.push({
  80:         line: y + 1, col: x + 1, type: 'corner',
  81:         msg: `Corner '${ch}' at (${y + 1}:${x + 1}) has no connections — misplaced or misaligned`
  82:       });
  83:     }
  84:   });
  85: 
  86:   // Check 3: Vertical pipe alignment
  87:   const pipeColumns = new Map();
  88:   lines.forEach((line, y) => {
  89:     for (let x = 0; x < line.length; x++) {
  90:       if (isVBorder(line[x])) {
  91:         if (!pipeColumns.has(x)) pipeColumns.set(x, []);
  92:         pipeColumns.get(x).push(y);
  93:       }
  94:     }
  95:   });
  96: 
  97:   pipeColumns.forEach((rows, col) => {
  98:     rows.sort((a, b) => a - b);
  99:     for (let i = 1; i < rows.length; i++) {
 100:       const gap = rows[i] - rows[i - 1];
 101:       if (gap === 2) {
 102:         const gapRow = rows[i - 1] + 1;
 103:         const gapChar = charAt(lines, col, gapRow);
 104:         // Allow corners, arrow heads, and other connectors in gap
 105:         if (gapChar === ' ') {
 106:           issues.push({
 107:             line: gapRow + 1, col: col + 1, type: 'broken-pipe',
 108:             msg: `Vertical pipe at column ${col + 1} has gap at line ${gapRow + 1} — possible misalignment`
 109:           });
 110:         }
 111:       }
 112:     }
 113:   });
 114: 
 115:   // Check 4: Arrow continuity — trace dash runs and check for internal gaps
 116:   lines.forEach((line, y) => {
 117:     // Find arrow heads and verify shaft
 118:     for (let x = 0; x < line.length; x++) {
 119:       if (ARROW_HEAD_R.has(line[x]) && x > 0) {
 120:         if (!ARROW_H.has(line[x - 1])) {
 121:           issues.push({
 122:             line: y + 1, col: x + 1, type: 'arrow',
 123:             msg: `Arrow head '${line[x]}' at (${y + 1}:${x + 1}) has no shaft to the left`
 124:           });
 125:         }
 126:       }
 127:       if (ARROW_HEAD_L.has(line[x]) && x + 1 < line.length) {
 128:         if (!ARROW_H.has(line[x + 1])) {
 129:           issues.push({
 130:             line: y + 1, col: x + 1, type: 'arrow',
 131:             msg: `Arrow head '${line[x]}' at (${y + 1}:${x + 1}) has no shaft to the right`
 132:           });
 133:         }
 134:       }
 135:     }
 136: 
 137:     // Find dash runs with internal gaps (e.g., "--- --->" is suspicious)
 138:     // Look for pattern: dashes, space(s), dashes on same line between two box borders
 139:     const dashGapPattern = /(-{2,})\s+(-{2,})/g;
 140:     let match;
 141:     while ((match = dashGapPattern.exec(line)) !== null) {
 142:       const gapStart = match.index + match[1].length;
 143:       const gapEnd = gapStart + match[0].length - match[1].length - match[2].length;
 144:       issues.push({
 145:         line: y + 1, col: gapStart + 1, type: 'broken-arrow',
 146:         msg: `Possible broken arrow at (${y + 1}:${gapStart + 1}) — dash run has internal gap`
 147:       });
 148:     }
 149:   });
 150: 
 151:   // Check 5: Vertical arrow continuity
 152:   // Find v/^ arrow heads and verify they have pipe above/below
 153:   // Skip characters that are part of words (e.g., 'v' in "Server", '^' in "car^et")
 154:   const isWordChar = (ch) => /[a-zA-Z0-9_]/.test(ch);
 155: 
 156:   lines.forEach((line, y) => {
 157:     for (let x = 0; x < line.length; x++) {
 158:       if (ARROW_HEAD_D.has(line[x])) {
 159:         // Skip if this character is part of a word
 160:         const left = x > 0 ? line[x - 1] : '';
 161:         const right = x + 1 < line.length ? line[x + 1] : '';
 162:         if (isWordChar(left) || isWordChar(right)) continue;
 163: 
 164:         const above = charAt(lines, x, y - 1);
 165:         if (!isVBorder(above) && above !== '+' && !isCorner(above)) {
 166:           issues.push({
 167:             line: y + 1, col: x + 1, type: 'arrow',
 168:             msg: `Down arrow '${line[x]}' at (${y + 1}:${x + 1}) has no shaft above`
 169:           });
 170:         }
 171:       }
 172:       if (ARROW_HEAD_U.has(line[x])) {
 173:         const left = x > 0 ? line[x - 1] : '';
 174:         const right = x + 1 < line.length ? line[x + 1] : '';
 175:         if (isWordChar(left) || isWordChar(right)) continue;
 176: 
 177:         const below = charAt(lines, x, y + 1);
 178:         if (!isVBorder(below) && below !== '+' && !isCorner(below)) {
 179:           issues.push({
 180:             line: y + 1, col: x + 1, type: 'arrow',
 181:             msg: `Up arrow '${line[x]}' at (${y + 1}:${x + 1}) has no shaft below`
 182:           });
 183:         }
 184:       }
 185:     }
 186:   });
 187: 
 188:   return {
 189:     valid: issues.length === 0,
 190:     issues,
 191:     stats: {
 192:       lines: lines.length,
 193:       maxWidth: Math.max(...lines.map(l => l.length)),
 194:       corners: corners.length,
 195:       pipeColumns: pipeColumns.size
 196:     }
 197:   };
 198: }
 199: 
 200: // CLI entry point
 201: if (require.main === module) {
 202:   let input;
 203:   const args = process.argv.slice(2);
 204: 
 205:   if (args.includes('--stdin')) {
 206:     input = fs.readFileSync(process.stdin.fd, 'utf8');
 207:   } else if (args.length > 0 && !args[0].startsWith('--')) {
 208:     input = fs.readFileSync(args[0], 'utf8');
 209:   } else {
 210:     console.error('Usage: node verify.cjs <file> | node verify.cjs --stdin');
 211:     process.exit(1);
 212:   }
 213: 
 214:   const result = verify(input);
 215:   console.log(JSON.stringify(result, null, 2));
 216:   process.exit(result.valid ? 0 : 1);
 217: }
 218: 
 219: module.exports = { verify };

 succeeded in 564ms:
   1: # Security
   2: 
   3: This document describes Citadel's security model and defensive measures against common attack vectors.
   4: 
   5: ## Threat Model
   6: 
   7: Citadel hooks run in the same security context as Claude Code itself. The primary threats are:
   8: 
   9: 1. **Path Traversal** — Malicious or confused agents attempting to access files outside the project root
  10: 2. **Shell Injection** — Command injection via unsanitized user/agent input in shell commands
  11: 3. **Secret Leakage** — Agents inadvertently reading .env files or other credential stores
  12: 4. **Untracked Dependencies** — Installing packages not declared in version control
  13: 
  14: ## Defenses
  15: 
  16: ### 1. Path Traversal Protection (`protect-files.js`)
  17: 
  18: All file operations (Read/Write/Edit) are validated before execution:
  19: 
  20: ```javascript
  21: // Blocks: ../../../etc/passwd
  22: // Blocks: /etc/passwd (absolute path outside project)
  23: // Allows: src/components/Button.tsx (relative to project root)
  24: ```
  25: 
  26: **Implementation:**
  27: - Regex-based detection of `../` and `..\` sequences
  28: - Absolute path validation against `PROJECT_ROOT`
  29: - Fail-closed design: unexpected errors block the action
  30: 
  31: **Test coverage:** `scripts/test-security.js` validates traversal attacks are blocked
  32: 
  33: ### 2. Shell Injection Prevention
  34: 
  35: All git operations use `execFileSync` with array arguments instead of shell strings:
  36: 
  37: ```javascript
  38: // ✅ Safe (no shell interpretation):
  39: execFileSync('git', ['rev-parse', 'HEAD'], { encoding: 'utf8' })
  40: 
  41: // ❌ Vulnerable (shell injection risk):
  42: execSync(`git rev-parse ${branch}`)  // if branch = "; rm -rf /"
  43: ```
  44: 
  45: **Files using execFileSync:**
  46: - `quality-gate.js` — git operations for lint checks
  47: - `harness-health-util.js` — git status queries
  48: - `post-edit.js` — file tracking
  49: 
  50: **Test coverage:** `scripts/test-security.js` verifies safe APIs are used
  51: 
  52: ### 3. Secret Protection
  53: 
  54: `.env` files and variants (`.env.local`, `.env.production`, etc.) are blocked from Read operations:
  55: 
  56: ```javascript
  57: // Blocks: .env, .env.local, .env.production
  58: // Allows: README.md, src/config.ts
  59: ```
  60: 
  61: **Rationale:** Prevents accidental credential leakage in agent conversations or logs.
  62: 
  63: **Test coverage:** `scripts/test-security.js` validates .env reads are blocked
  64: 
  65: ### 4. Pip Gate (Untracked Dependencies)
  66: 
  67: Before allowing `pip install`, checks if `requirements.txt` is tracked by git:
  68: 
  69: ```javascript
  70: // ✅ Allows: pip install when requirements.txt is committed
  71: // ⚠️  Warns:  pip install when requirements.txt exists but is untracked
  72: // ✅ Allows: pip install when no requirements.txt exists yet
  73: ```
  74: 
  75: **Rationale:** Prevents dependency drift and ensures reproducible builds.
  76: 
  77: **Implementation:** `quality-gate.js` checks `git ls-files requirements.txt`
  78: 
  79: ## Test Suite
  80: 
  81: ### Run Security Tests
  82: 
  83: ```bash
  84: # Security tests only
  85: node scripts/test-security.js
  86: 
  87: # Full suite (includes security)
  88: node scripts/test-all.js
  89: ```
  90: 
  91: ### What Gets Tested
  92: 
  93: 1. **Path Traversal**
  94:    - `../../../etc/passwd` → blocked
  95:    - `/etc/passwd` → blocked
  96:    - `src/file.ts` → allowed
  97: 
  98: 2. **Shell Injection**
  99:    - Verify `execFileSync` usage (safe)
 100:    - Reject `execSync` usage (unsafe)
 101:    - Validate git commands use array args
 102: 
 103: 3. **Secret Protection**
 104:    - `.env` reads → blocked
 105:    - Regular file reads → allowed
 106: 
 107: 4. **Glob Pattern Security**
 108:    - `secrets/**` patterns work correctly
 109:    - Recursive `**` globs match expected files
 110: 
 111: ### Exit Codes
 112: 
 113: - `0` — All tests pass
 114: - `1` — One or more tests failed (DO NOT SHIP)
 115: 
 116: ## Fail-Closed Design
 117: 
 118: All security hooks follow a **fail-closed** approach:
 119: 
 120: - Unexpected errors → **block** the action (exit 2)
 121: - Parse failures → **block** the action
 122: - Missing validation → **block** the action
 123: 
 124: This prevents security bypasses via error conditions.
 125: 
 126: ## Reporting Vulnerabilities
 127: 
 128: If you discover a security issue:
 129: 
 130: 1. **Do not** open a public GitHub issue
 131: 2. Email: security@citadel.dev (or create private security advisory)
 132: 3. Include: steps to reproduce, impact assessment, suggested fix
 133: 
 134: We'll respond within 48 hours and coordinate disclosure timing.
 135: 
 136: ## Audit Log
 137: 
 138: All security-relevant events are logged to `.planning/telemetry/audit.jsonl`:
 139: 
 140: ```json
 141: {
 142:   "schema": 1,
 143:   "event": "blocked",
 144:   "hook": "protect-files",
 145:   "reason": "path traversal sequence",
 146:   "file": "../../../etc/passwd",
 147:   "timestamp": "2026-03-30T12:05:00.000Z"
 148: }
 149: ```
 150: 
 151: Use this log for forensic analysis and security monitoring.
 152: 
 153: ## Security Checklist for New Hooks
 154: 
 155: When adding a new hook that handles user/agent input:
 156: 
 157: - [ ] Use `health.validatePath()` for all file paths
 158: - [ ] Use `health.validateCommand()` for all shell commands
 159: - [ ] Use `execFileSync` with array args (not `execSync` or `exec`)
 160: - [ ] Fail closed: unexpected errors exit 2 (block), not 0 (allow)
 161: - [ ] Add test cases to `scripts/test-security.js`
 162: - [ ] Document security assumptions in hook header comment
 163: 
 164: ## Further Reading
 165: 
 166: - [OWASP Path Traversal](https://owasp.org/www-community/attacks/Path_Traversal)
 167: - [Shell Injection Prevention](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/OS_Command_Injection_Defense_Cheat_Sheet.html)
 168: - [Principle of Least Privilege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principle_of_least_privilege)

  35: function extractHookName(command) {
  36:   const match = command.match(/hooks_src\/([^.]+)\.js/);
  37:   return match ? match[1] : null;
  38: }
  39: 
  40: function translateCodexHooks(hooksTemplate, adapterScriptPath) {
  41:   const codexHooks = {};
  42:   const warnings = [];
  43:   const installed = [];
  44:   const skipped = [];
  45:   const adapterPath = adapterScriptPath.replace(/\\/g, '/');
  46:   const adapterCmd = quoteNodeCommand(`node ${adapterPath}`);
  47: 
  48:   for (const [citadelEvent, entries] of Object.entries(hooksTemplate.hooks || {})) {
  49:     const codexEvent = EVENT_MAP[citadelEvent];
  50: 
  51:     if (!codexEvent) {
  52:       for (const entry of entries) {
  53:         for (const hook of entry.hooks || []) {
  54:           const name = extractHookName(hook.command);
  55:           if (name) skipped.push({ hook: name, event: citadelEvent, reason: 'no Codex equivalent' });
  56:         }
  57:       }
  58:       warnings.push(`${citadelEvent}: no Codex equivalent (${entries.length} hook(s) skipped)`);
  59:       continue;
  60:     }
  61: 
  62:     if (!codexHooks[codexEvent]) codexHooks[codexEvent] = [];
  63: 
  64:     for (const entry of entries) {
  65:       if (!entry.hooks) continue;
  66: 
  67:       const hooks = [];
  68:       for (const hook of entry.hooks) {
  69:         const hookName = extractHookName(hook.command);
  70:         if (!hookName) continue;
  71:         hooks.push({
  72:           type: 'command',
  73:           command: `${adapterCmd} ${hookName}`,
  74:           statusMessage: `Citadel: ${hookName}`,
  75:           timeout: hook.timeout || 30,
  76:         });
  77:         installed.push({ hook: hookName, event: codexEvent });
  78:       }
  79: 
  80:       if (hooks.length === 0) continue;
  81: 
  82:       // Expand pipe-delimited matchers into separate entries (e.g. "Edit|Write" → two entries)
  83:       const matchers = entry.matcher ? entry.matcher.split('|').map((m) => m.trim()).filter(Boolean) : [null];
  84:       for (const matcher of matchers) {
  85:         const codexEntry = { hooks };
  86:         if (matcher) codexEntry.matcher = matcher;
  87:         codexHooks[codexEvent].push(codexEntry);
  88:       }
  89:     }
  90:   }
  91: 
  92:   return { hooks: codexHooks, installed, skipped, warnings };
  93: }
  94: 
  95: function installCodexHooks(options = {}) {
  96:   const existingHooks = options.existingHooks || {};
  97:   const translated = translateCodexHooks(options.hooksTemplate, options.adapterScriptPath);
  98:   const mergedHooks = mergeHookMaps({
  99:     existingHooks,
 100:     generatedHooks: translated.hooks,
 101:     preserveMarker: 'codex-adapter',
 102:   });
 103: 
 104:   const filteredHooks = Object.fromEntries(
 105:     Object.entries(mergedHooks).filter(([event]) => CODEX_EVENTS.has(event))
 106:   );
 107: 
 108:   if (options.outputPath) {
 109:     writeJson(options.outputPath, { hooks: filteredHooks });
 110:   }
 111: 
 112:   return {
 113:     ...translated,
 114:     hooks: filteredHooks,
 115:   };
 116: }

  18: 
  19: ## What Is Citadel
  20: 
  21: An agent orchestration harness for Claude Code and OpenAI Codex. It coordinates multiple AI agents in parallel, persists memory across sessions, and routes your intent to the cheapest execution path automatically. Citadel adapts itself to each runtime: plugin-first for Claude Code, generated project artifacts plus Codex-native config for Codex.
  22: 
  23: ## Why Citadel Exists
  24: 
  25: **Without Citadel**, every agent session starts from zero. You re-explain architecture decisions. You re-discover that the auth module is fragile. You copy-paste the same review checklist. When a task is too big for one agent, you manually split it and lose context between the pieces. Your agents never get better at your codebase — you just get better at prompting them.
  26: 
  27: **With Citadel**, sessions resume where they left off. A `/do review` runs a structured 5-pass review that remembers what broke last time. A `/do overhaul the API layer` spawns parallel agents in isolated worktrees, shares discoveries between them, and merges the results. Skills you build once compound across every future session. The system learns from its own mistakes through campaign persistence and telemetry.
  28: 
  29: The difference: `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` tell the runtime about your project. Citadel gives the runtime the *infrastructure to work autonomously* — routing, memory, safety hooks, and coordination that a single guidance file can't provide.
  30: 
  31: ## Quickstart
  32: 
  33: **Prerequisites:** [Claude Code](https://docs.anthropic.com/en/docs/claude-code) or [Codex](https://github.com/openai/codex) + [Node.js 18+](https://nodejs.org/)
  34: 
  35: ```bash
  36: # 1. Clone Citadel
  37: git clone https://github.com/SethGammon/Citadel.git
  38: 
  39: # 2a. Claude Code runtime
  40: claude --plugin-dir /path/to/Citadel
  41: 
  42: # 2b. Codex runtime
  43: node /path/to/Citadel/scripts/codex-compat.js
  44: node /path/to/Citadel/scripts/install-hooks-codex.js
  45: codex
  46: 
  47: # 3. In either runtime, run setup
  48: /do setup
  49: 
  50: # 4. Try something
  51: /do review src/main.ts
  52: ```
  53: 
  54: [Quickstart for both runtimes](QUICKSTART.md) · [Claude Code installation guide](docs/CLAUDE_INSTALLATION_GUIDE.md) · [Codex installation guide](docs/CODEX_INSTALLATION_GUIDE.md)
  55: 
  56: ## How It Works
  57: 
  58: Say what you want. `/do` routes it to the cheapest tool that can handle it.
  59: 
  60: ```
  61: /do fix the typo on line 42        # Direct edit, no model call
  62: /do review the auth module         # 5-pass structured code review
  63: /do why is the API returning 500   # Root cause analysis
  64: /do build a caching layer          # Multi-step orchestrated build
  65: /do overhaul all three services    # Parallel fleet with isolated worktrees
  66: ```
  67: 
  68: Classification runs across four tiers, each cheaper than the last:
  69: 
  70: 1. **Pattern match** — catches trivial commands with regex. Zero tokens, zero model calls, instant.
  71: 2. **Session state** — checks if you're mid-campaign and resumes it. Still zero tokens.
  72: 3. **Keyword lookup** — scans your input against installed skill keywords ("review", "test", "refactor") and routes directly. Still zero tokens.
  73: 4. **LLM classification** — only when tiers 1-3 don't match, a structured complexity analysis (~500 tokens) determines whether you need a single-step Marshal, a multi-session Archon, or a parallel Fleet.
  74: 
  75: Most requests resolve at tiers 1-3 for free. Tier 4 is the exception, not the default. You never have to choose the tool.
  76: 
  77: **[See it route live](https://sethgammon.github.io/Citadel/)**
  78: 
  79: ## The Orchestration Ladder
  80: 
  81: Four tiers. Use the cheapest one that fits.
  82: 
  83: <table>
  84: <tr>
  85: <td width="50%">

  98: </td>
  99: </tr>
 100: </table>
 101: 
 102: ## What You Get
 103: 
 104: **Cost transparency.** Citadel reads runtime-native session artifacts and computes real cost from API pricing. You see what every session, campaign, and agent actually costs. Use `/cost` for a full breakdown or `/dashboard` for the overview. A real-time tracker alerts you at configurable spend thresholds without interrupting your work.
 105: 
 106: **Safety hooks.** 32 hooks across 29 lifecycle events run automatically. A consent system gates external actions (pushes, PRs, comments) with first-encounter choice — always-ask, session-allow, or auto-allow. Protected branches can't be deleted. Path traversal and secrets exfiltration are blocked. A circuit breaker stops failure spirals before they burn tokens. All of this is configurable per-project in `harness.json`.
 107: 
 108: **Campaign persistence.** Multi-session work survives context compression and session boundaries. Start an architecture overhaul today, close your laptop, pick it up tomorrow — the campaign state, decisions, and progress are all preserved. `/do continue` resumes exactly where you left off.
 109: 
 110: **Parallel coordination.** Fleet mode spawns multiple agents in isolated git worktrees, shares discoveries between them in real time, and merges results. One command, multiple agents, no conflicts.
 111: 
 112: **Autonomous quality improvement.** `/evolve` is a research-driven improvement director that scores the harness against a rubric, forms causal hypotheses about why scores are low, validates them with scout agents before spending fleet budget, and runs improvement cycles until it hits a ceiling or budget. It accumulates a persistent belief model and transferable pattern library across sessions — so each run compounds on what prior runs learned.
 113: 
 114: ## FAQ
 115: 
 116: **Is this for me?** If you're running Claude Code or Codex on a real codebase and finding that agents lose context, repeat mistakes, or can't work in parallel, yes. If you're just starting out with either runtime, get a few sessions in first and come back when the friction shows up.
 117: 
 118: **How is this different from `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md`?** Those files tell the runtime about your project. Citadel tells the runtime *how to work*: durable state, intelligent routing, automated safety, and native parallelism — the infrastructure layer those files assume someone else built.
 119: 
 120: **Do I need to learn all 45 skills?** No. Just use `/do` and describe what you want in plain English. The router picks the right skill. You can go months without ever typing a skill name directly.
 121: 
 122: **What if `/do` routes to the wrong tool?** Tell it. "Wrong tool" or "just do it yourself" and it adjusts. You can also invoke any skill directly: `/review`, `/archon`, etc. The router is a convenience, not a gate.
 123: 
 124: **How much does it cost in tokens?** Citadel adds ~2.5% overhead to your session cost. Skills cost zero when not loaded. The `/do` router costs ~500 tokens only at Tier 4. Use `/cost` to see real token data and exact spend for any session or campaign.
 125: 
 126: **How is this different from CrewAI, LangChain, or Aider?** Those are agent frameworks: they give you primitives for building agents from scratch. Citadel is an *operating system for an existing agent* (Claude Code or Codex). You don't write agent code — you install a plugin and get routing, persistence, parallelism, and safety hooks on top of the agent you already use. If you're building a custom agent, use a framework. If you're using Claude Code or Codex and want it to work better, use Citadel.
 127: 
 128: **Does it work with OpenAI Codex?** Yes. The runtime layer (`packages/runtime-claude-code`, `packages/contracts`) abstracts over both runtimes. Skills, hooks, and campaigns are portable — the same `.planning/` state works whether you're running under Claude Code or Codex. Use `AGENTS.md` for Codex (parallel to `CLAUDE.md` for Claude Code).
 129: 
 130: **Does this work on Windows?** Yes. All hooks and scripts run on Node.js. The Claude plugin path and the Codex generated-artifact path both work cross-platform.
 131:
